| **Cache-corruption bugfix (ADR-014 follow-up)** | ✅ Fixed in all 4 known call sites, **verified live against a real running server for each one** | Found while building the two rows above: this app's default `database` cache store (`CACHE_STORE=database`) corrupts a cached value on the next read if it still has any real object nested inside it — a raw Eloquent Collection/Model, or even just one raw `Carbon` date buried inside an otherwise-plain array — surfacing as a broken `{"__PHP_Incomplete_Class_Name": ...}` JSON body instead of an exception. **Never caught by `php artisan test`** — `phpunit.xml` sets `CACHE_STORE=array` (in-memory, never actually serializes), so this bug was invisible to the entire existing test suite no matter how many times it ran. Fixed: `GameController::index()`/`packages()` (pre-existing, admin Games & Packages — this ADR-014's own original implementation), `Middleware\PaymentMethodController::index()` (pre-existing, same original implementation — found in a follow-up docs-accuracy sweep, not the initial fix pass), `CatalogController::index()` (`created_at` was a raw Carbon instance), `HeroSlideController::index()` (designed correctly from the start once understood). Full details, root cause, and the fix pattern (`->toArray()` before caching, or explicit `?->toISOString()` per date field) are in [ADR-014's newest addendum](./adr.md) — not duplicated here. **Consequence to track:** any future `Cache::remember()` call in this codebase must cache a plain array from the start, or it will reproduce this exact bug the first time it's actually cache-hit outside the test suite | Regression test added to each of the 4 controllers' test files, each forcing `config(['cache.default' => 'database'])` — the suite's default `array` driver structurally cannot catch this class of bug |

**Manual smoke-test tools** (not part of the automated suite — deliberately hit real external APIs): `php artisan app:xendit-smoke-test` and `php artisan app:gamevion-smoke-test` (`app/Console/Commands/Smoke/`). Xendit run confirmed against the real sandbox: Basic Auth accepted, sen→ringgit conversion accepted, and three rounds of real `API_VALIDATION_ERROR` responses parsed correctly by `XenditGateway`'s error normalization — proves the adapter plumbing works end-to-end. Stopped short of a full `201 Created` only because `DUITNOW_PAY`'s exact `issuer_code` values aren't known yet (needs Xendit Dashboard, not a code question). **Gamevion smoke test run 2026-07-25 against the real live API — succeeded** (see Supplier Adapter layer row above for detail).

**Security review, 2026-07-24:** ran a full manual audit of all backend money-critical code (no `origin` remote configured, so the `/security-review` skill's git-diff step couldn't run — reviewed by hand instead). Found and fixed 4 issues, most severely a **critical** one: `OrderFulfillmentService::fulfill()` had no row lock, so two near-simultaneous calls for the same Order (e.g. a duplicate webhook delivery — explicitly expected behavior per Xendit's own docs, exactly what PAY-2 exists to prevent) could each generate a *different* `reference_number` and submit two separate orders to Gamevion, double-delivering game credits and double-crediting the ledger for one payment. Fixed with `DB::transaction()` + `lockForUpdate()`, mirroring `LedgerService::withdraw()`/`VoucherService::redeem()`'s existing pattern exactly, and proven with a new real two-OS-process test (`tests/Concurrency/OrderFulfillmentConcurrencyTest.php`, backed by `app:order-fulfillment-test-fulfill` in `app/Console/Commands/Testing/`) — not just trusted by inspection. Also fixed: `CheckoutService::initiate()` no longer wraps the outbound Xendit call in a DB transaction (previously risked orphaning a real, payable Xendit payment link with no matching Order if the transaction failed to commit after Xendit had already accepted the request — now the Order commits first, so the only failure mode is the safe direction: an Order stuck at Pending with no `payment_ref`); `OrderFulfillmentService` now fails fast with a new `OrderFulfillmentException` when `supplier_product_ref` is missing, instead of silently sending an empty `product_code` to the supplier; `CheckoutTotalService::calculate()` now rejects a negative `voucher_discount` instead of letting it inflate `final_amount` above the correct price.

| **First real end-to-end order (MLBB Malaysia) + `GamevionAdapter` `telp` fix + duplicate-promote guard** | ✅ Payment half proven live; delivery half blocked on Gamevion's sandbox (not ours, see [ADR-006's newest addendum](./adr.md)) | Founder walked through the platform's actual first real order, live: linked MLBB (Malaysia)'s `validation_rules` to `zone_id` (correct for MLBB — User ID + Zone ID), promoted a real cheap item ("13 + 1 Diamonds", GV572) via the Product Manager API directly — which **surfaced a real gap the founder caught**: `SupplierProductController::promote()` had zero backend guard against promoting the same `(supplier_id, supplier_package_ref)` twice (the Product Manager frontend only hides the "Add Again" button client-side once a raw item shows promoted — calling the API directly bypasses that entirely, and did, creating two Packages for the same GV572 item). Fixed with the same defense-in-depth pattern as `vouchers.order_id` (migration `2026_07_25_140000`): new unique index `(supplier_id, supplier_package_ref)` on `packages` (migration `2026_07_25_180000_add_unique_index_to_packages_supplier_ref.php`) plus a friendly `ValidationException` in `promote()` ahead of it. **MySQL-specific migration gotcha hit and fixed**: the original `packages` table's `supplier_id` foreign key had no dedicated backing index of its own on live MySQL (confirmed via `SHOW INDEX`) — adding the composite unique without also adding a standalone `supplier_id` index made MySQL depend on the *unique* index to satisfy the FK, which then blocked dropping that unique index on any future rollback (MySQL error 1553). Fixed by adding an explicit standalone `supplier_id` index in the same migration, and writing `down()` to only reverse the unique constraint (the standalone index is a permanent correction, not something to undo) — verified by actually running the migration against the dockerized MySQL and the full concurrency suite, not just sqlite. Then ran the real chain: `POST /api/checkout` (real User ID `51049607`/Server ID `2005`) → real Xendit `201` → manually simulated the Xendit webhook (curl, same shape Xendit's own docs specify) → `payment_status` reached `paid` → `OrderFulfillmentService` called the real `GamevionAdapter`, which surfaced the `telp` bug (see its own note in ADR-006's addendum) — fixed, retried, and hit the sandbox-side `500` (also in that addendum). All throwaway test data (2 Orders, 1 duplicate Package, 1 smoke-test AdminUser) cleaned up afterward; the real MLBB Package and its `validation_rules` were kept as genuine catalog data | `tests/Feature/Services/Supplier/GamevionAdapterTest.php` (+2: strips non-digit `telp` characters, omits `telp` entirely when no phone given), `tests/Feature/Http/Controllers/Middleware/SupplierProductControllerTest.php` (+1: rejects an already-promoted item) |

**Test suite total (2026-07-27 checkpoint, end of Order Resend Delivery build session): 396 passing** in the default sqlite suite + 4 in the MySQL concurrency suite (unchanged this session). Grew from 361 (the ADR-016 shipped checkpoint) by 35 across three sessions: Image Gallery (+8), `CheckoutController` player-ID enforcement (+6), and this session's Order Resend Delivery (+21: `OrderResendServiceTest` 12, `ResendOrderDeliveryJobTest` 4, `OrderControllerTest` +5 resend cases). Storefront itself (`storefront/`) has no automated test suite — verified by hand against the real running backend instead (see its own §14 row above). Update this line again next time it's touched rather than trusting the old number.

**Storefront auth decision, 2026-07-24:** confirmed via live comparison (browser) that keroxshop.com (our own site) is 100% guest-checkout — no login/register anywhere, only "Track Order" by order number. gamevion.com does have customer accounts, but for a reason that doesn't apply to us: their site runs a customer wallet (pre-funded balance) plus a reseller/membership-tier program, both of which require persistent identity. Our PRD has no Customer entity and no customer-facing wallet concept — Order already only stores `customer_email`/`customer_phone` as plain strings. Decision: MVP storefront stays guest-checkout; auth (Sanctum) is for `AdminUser` only. Revisit only if a customer wallet/loyalty feature is deliberately added later (would be its own ADR-level decision, not a default).

---

**Admin Panel login flow, end-to-end, 2026-07-24:** the four pieces flagged in the previous session's pointer (below, kept for history) are all built and — unlike everything above, which is proven by `phpunit` — this slice was verified by an actual human logging in through a real browser against a real Herd-served backend. That surfaced three local-environment gotchas worth recording so the next session doesn't rediscover them the hard way:
- `backend/config/cors.php` **did not exist**. Laravel's `HandleCors` middleware only matches paths from `config('cors.paths')`, which resolves to nothing if the app never published this file — so every cross-origin call from `admin/` was silently getting zero CORS headers, not "misconfigured" CORS. Created it explicitly (`allowed_origins` from an `ADMIN_URL` env var, default `http://localhost:3000`; `supports_credentials: false`, since auth is Bearer-token per ADR-009, not cookie-based Sanctum SPA auth — `SANCTUM_STATEFUL_DOMAINS` was a red herring, never actually needed).
- Herd serves this backend at a per-developer domain (e.g. `kedairuncit-backend.test`, from parking the repo folder — not the generic `backend.test` the code defaults to), and **force-redirects `http` → `https`** for secured sites. A redirect on a `POST` silently downgrades to `GET` and drops the body — so `admin/.env.local`'s `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` must point at the `https://` origin directly, never rely on following the redirect.
- Node (unlike the OS/browser) doesn't trust Herd's locally-installed CA by default, so a plain `fetch()` to that `https://*.test` origin from a Route Handler throws `UNABLE_TO_VERIFY_LEAF_SIGNATURE`. Fixed with `NODE_OPTIONS=--use-system-ca` prepended to `admin/package.json`'s `dev` script (Node ≥22 flag, confirmed present on Node v24) — makes Node trust the same system CA store the browser already does, instead of chasing the CA file path by hand.

`admin/.env.local` is gitignored and per-developer (holds each dev's own Herd domain) — not assumed as a shared default anywhere in code.

**AUTH-4 + TailAdmin shell, 2026-07-24:** Admin Users (create/edit/activate/deactivate) is the first complete vertical slice — backend, route, and a real UI screen, not just an API. The admin panel's visual foundation also landed this session: components ported from the free TailAdmin Next.js template (see [ADR-012](./adr.md) — **UI/UX reference and component source only**, no business data or feature assumptions from it apply to this project, same discipline as [`legacy-reference-notes.md`](./legacy-reference-notes.md)). Decision on how to build screens going forward: **wire per-feature, not "mockup everything then wire"** — adopt the template's UI shell, define the TypeScript data contract, and wire directly to the real backend endpoint in the same pass whenever that endpoint already exists or is simple to add (as with AUTH-4); only stub with placeholder data when the backend shape is still genuinely undecided, and swap it for the real fetch before moving on, not as a separate later phase.

**Withdrawal management, 2026-07-24:** WTH-1..5 done end-to-end (backend + UI), confirming §15's point that most admin-facing money-adjacent features aren't actually blocked on the supplier decision — only the catalog-dependent ones are. Founder-confirmed decisions for this feature (see the WTH-1..5 row above for detail): Admin Panel gets its own "Request Withdrawal" action (no reseller portal exists yet to originate one), maker-checker only kicks in at/above RM 2,000, and Reject is Pending-only for MVP (no reverse-debit flow).

**xenPlatform sub-account type decided, 2026-07-24 — later reversed.** Researched Xendit's OWNED vs. MANAGED docs directly; founder chose OWNED. **Superseded 2026-08-30 ([ADR-059](./adr.md) decision 6): xenPlatform dropped entirely.** The one consequence that carried over regardless: the `Withdrawal` model's bank-detail fields are the right shape for reseller payouts (now a plain manual bank transfer).

**Voucher management, 2026-07-24:** VCH-1..6 done. This one revealed the Withdrawal-round questions don't transfer as-is — the founder clarified vouchers actually have **two distinct creation paths** with different risk profiles (see the VCH-1..6 row above): a standalone admin-chosen-amount path (threshold-gated, RM 500) and an order-triggered auto-computed-refund path (never threshold-gated, since the amount is bounded by what the customer paid, not an open choice). Worth remembering for any future feature that "looks like" an already-built one — verify the shape again, don't assume the pattern from the last feature carries over unchanged.

**Gamevion goes live + Game/Package model, 2026-07-25:** the Gamevion blocker is resolved — real `GAMEVION_BEARER_TOKEN`/`GAMEVION_API_KEY` obtained (dashboard's single "API Key" field turned out to be the `X-API-KEY` value only; the `Authorization: Bearer` secret is a separate credential, also now in hand), smoke test succeeded against the live API. Also stood up a generic, supplier-agnostic outbound proxy seam (`config/services.php['proxy']`, VPS-backed) for any *future* supplier needing IP whitelisting — not needed for Gamevion itself, built ahead of time since the dev machine has no static IP. With real catalog access confirmed (316 products / 22 categories, clean data), built `Game`/`Package` model + migration per PRD §8 — deliberately model-and-migration-only (see that row above), same incremental discipline as `Supplier`. Also resolved a TODO left in the original `orders` migration: `game_id`/`package_id`/`supplier_id` now have real FK constraints (`nullOnDelete()`).

**Price Sync design session + Stage 1 build, 2026-07-25 (later same day):** before writing any code, did a live comparative walkthrough of `manage.keroxshop.com` (Product Manager, Price List, Games — read-only, no sync/add/remove actions taken) and `keroxshop.com`'s real storefront, specifically to answer: does syncing a supplier's catalog make it instantly live, and how does the legacy system handle two suppliers offering "the same" package at different prices? Confirmed via the legacy system's own `/api/supplier/games/1/compare-packages` endpoint (flat per-supplier lists, no `matched_with`/`group_id` field) plus a live before/after on `keroxshop.com/game/mobile-legends-malaysia`: **package-level "same product, different supplier" resolution happens at storefront query time, by exact display-name string match, picking `MIN(price)` — not a stored grouping, and not enforced at the admin/catalog layer** (the admin Catalog table there happily keeps multiple same-named entries "active" simultaneously; only the storefront query collapses them). Also confirmed the legacy `Game` row's own schema is hardcoded to exactly two suppliers (`moogold_product_id`/`barbar_product_id` as dedicated columns, not a list) — a real limitation this project's `Game.supplier_mappings` JSON array already avoids. Founder-confirmed decision: **defer any multi-supplier product-grouping schema entirely** — the cheapest-by-name-at-query-time behavior needs no schema beyond what already exists (`Package.name`, `reseller_cost_price`), so it can be added as pure query logic in `CheckoutController`/storefront listing whenever that's built, supplier-count-agnostic from day one, no migration ever required for it.

Built **Price Sync Stage 1** (raw catalog mirror) same session — see its own row above (`ProductSyncService`, `supplier_products` table, `app:sync-supplier-products` command). Run twice against the live Gamevion API for real (not just test fakes): 316 products, `created: 316 → updated: 316` on the second run, proving the upsert.

**Price Sync Stage 2, 2026-07-25 (later still same day):** built and shipped — see its own row above. Real `Package` rows now exist (created via a real browser session, not a seeder) — `CheckoutController`'s blocker is close to resolved but not fully: it needs *enough* curated `Package` rows for a real order flow, not just proof the promote path works for one item.

**Price Sync Stage 2 UX revision, 2026-07-25 (same day, right after the first version shipped):** founder feedback on the flat 316-item list — Package (denomination) and Game shouldn't be conflated, and re-picking the Game for every single item is unnecessary busywork when it's really a per-category decision (~22 categories, not 316 items). Restructured to a two-level category-group view; also clarified in the same conversation that "does every package have its own identifying slug" meant Gamevion's own `product_code`, not a URL slug — confirmed already captured end-to-end, just not visibly displayed (now is). See the Stage 2 row above for the full rebuild.

**Product Manager polish, 2026-07-25 (same day, after further founder review):** two more UX issues caught by the founder actually using the built screen, not by testing: **(1)** the "Add Again" button on an already-promoted item was a real bug, not a feature — clicking it created a *second, separate* `Package` row pointing at the same Gamevion item, i.e. two live packages selling identical inventory, possibly at different prices, with no legitimate use case. Removed — an already-promoted row now shows only its status, no action. A proper "Edit Package" (rename/re-price an existing Package) is deliberately deferred as its own feature, not bundled in. **(2)** The promote modal pre-filled "Reseller Cost Price" with the exact same number as the supplier cost, which looked like admin was editing the cost price itself (backend already never accepted that as input — `cost_price` has only ever come from `SupplierProduct::price_sen`, never the form) — fixed by showing supplier cost as a separate read-only line and leaving the reseller-price field blank, with a one-line explanation of what it means.

**Price Sync feature scope confirmed (not built yet), 2026-07-25:** the founder's question about cost-price editability surfaced a real, larger gap — `Package.cost_price` today is a **one-time snapshot** taken at promote time; if Gamevion changes a price and Stage 1 is re-run, `supplier_products.price_sen` updates but the already-promoted `Package.cost_price` does not. Founder confirmed this should be fixed, but explicitly as part of a dedicated **Price Sync** feature (§6.4 SYNC-1..6 — Admin Price Sync Center), not bolted onto Stage 1/2 as an incidental patch. Scoped for that future feature, all from the same conversation: **(a)** propagate a re-synced `price_sen` change into any already-promoted `Package.cost_price` (never touching admin-set `reseller_cost_price`, and never touching historical `Order` snapshots, which are already independent per ORD-9); **(b)** detect when a supplier turns a previously-active item off, and route it through something like the SYNC-5/6 "Pending Reactivation" review pattern rather than silently leaving a now-dead package live; **(c)** currency conversion — if a future supplier bills in a currency other than MYR, Price Sync needs to convert before storing/displaying, which nothing in the codebase handles today (Gamevion itself is MYR, so this hasn't been a live issue yet, but the feature should be built with it in mind, not retrofitted later).

**Admin — Games & Packages built, 2026-07-25 (later still same day):** built and verified live — see its own row above. Closes the founder-raised workflow gap directly: `/middleware/product-manager` (promote) → `/admin/games` (review/edit/deactivate/delete) is now a real, working end-to-end loop, not just the promote half of it.

Not investigated or fixed — flagging so a future session doesn't mistake it for a regression from `/admin/games`.

- **Live end-to-end checkout — payment half fully proven, delivery half blocked on Gamevion, not us. START HERE NEXT SESSION.** Ran the platform's first-ever real order (MLBB Malaysia, real User ID/Server ID) all the way through: `POST /api/checkout` → real Xendit `201` → simulated webhook → `payment_status: paid` → real `GamevionAdapter::createOrder()` call. Found and fixed the `telp`-format bug along the way (see its own §14 row + [ADR-006's addendum](./adr.md)), then hit a **Gamevion-side sandbox limitation**: `/api/order` returns a generic `500` for any real product in sandbox mode, while the identical request against **production** returns a clean, correct `400 Insufficient balance` — conclusively proving the request/adapter is correct and the bug is Gamevion's sandbox environment, not ours. **Follow-up same day, 2026-07-25:** confirmed there is no workaround — the sandbox-only `product_code` returned by `/api/product`'s `sandbox` flag (`234`/`mlbb5`, labelled `"Products Simulation (Sandbox)"` by Gamevion itself) is rejected as `422 "invalid product code"` when actually ordered, and re-testing the real `GV572` code reproduced the identical `500` again — see [ADR-006's newest addendum](./adr.md) for the raw JSON evidence. **Next session:** (1) follow up with Gamevion support using the full diagnostic evidence trail already gathered (don't re-derive it — it's in the ADR-006 addendums); (2) once sandbox order creation works again, re-run the exact same live chain to get delivery to actually succeed (this genuinely closes the full pipeline, first time ever); (3) decide whether `GAMEVION_SANDBOX=false` against production is ever acceptable for a deliberate one-off real test (spends real money, delivers to a real player account — not a casual decision); (4) **Player/server ID validation — addressed 2026-07-25 (new session), but not the way this bullet originally framed it.** Gamevion itself still has no validation endpoint and it's still unconfirmed how an invalid ID surfaces when Gamevion actually processes an order (that half of this question is genuinely still open — see ADR-005/ADR-006). What got built instead is an **independent third-party validation layer** for MLBB specifically (three unofficial provider APIs, fallback chain, admin Validators screen — see the Player-ID Validation row in this table and [ADR-005's newest addendum](./adr.md)) — a real capability, but a different mechanism than "wait for Gamevion to reveal it." Storefront/checkout wiring for this new layer is itself the next open item — see the fresh NEXT SESSION note below.
- **Real bug also found and fixed this session, unrelated to payment/Gamevion**: `SupplierProductController::promote()` had no backend guard against promoting the same supplier item twice — fixed with a DB unique index + friendly validation error. See its own §14 row for the MySQL migration gotcha this surfaced too (a pre-existing missing index on `packages.supplier_id`, now fixed).
- ~~**Storefront app** (§6.16) is still the single biggest remaining gap — no Next.js app scaffolded at all.~~ **Superseded, 2026-07-26** — `storefront/` now exists with 4 pages live; see its own §14 row and the newest NEXT SESSION pointer at the end of this section.
- **Middleware Test Order tool (MID-9/MUI-8) + Request Logs (MID-10/11/MUI-9) — researched, not built.** Founder asked for this after seeing the legacy `manage.keroxshop.com/orders` screen, then independently caught that its own "Test Orders" table is real orders and test orders mixed together with no distinction — exactly the failure mode to design around, not copy. Conclusions reached (see the design-discussion note below for full reasoning), not yet implemented:
  1. A test order attempt must **never** write to the `orders` table — it exercises `SupplierAdapter::createOrder()` directly (bypassing `CheckoutService`/pricing/payment entirely), matching the PRD's own pre-existing "Dry Run" glossary definition ("without creating real orders or deducting balance").
  2. The history of test *and* production supplier-API calls belongs in Request Logs (MID-10/11), a single log with a `triggered_by: production|test` distinction — not a bespoke test-orders table, which would just reproduce the legacy mixing problem one layer down.
  3. Not every supplier will have a sandbox like Gamevion does — proposed a `Supplier.supports_sandbox` boolean (same pattern as GAME-12's per-mapping `supports_validation`) so the tool can fall back to "construct and show the request, never send it" for suppliers without one.
  This is real, useful tooling but doesn't touch the money pipeline — deliberately sequenced after Orders/Storefront, not before.
- Bulk-curate more of the 316-item Gamevion catalog into real `Package`s as needed (`/middleware/product-manager`) — admin busywork, not a code blocker.
- Once the Storefront (or this design) exists, wire Voucher Path B's existing backend endpoint (`POST /orders/{order}/voucher`) to a real "Create Voucher" button on the order detail view — `/admin/orders`'s detail screen is the natural home for it, not built in this pass (ORD-7 scope).
- Other not-blocked areas per §15 (MFA; Backups; Settings; Reports) remain fair game whenever prioritized. **Update, 2026-07-29: Blacklist/Fraud and Image Gallery, both listed here originally, are now ✅ done — see §15.** MFA (AUTH-7) is explicitly descoped as of 2026-07-29 (founder's own call, not silently forgotten — see the Auth row in §15 and ADR-019's newest addendum) rather than still "open": full TOTP enrollment+verify is a real feature build, not started.
- **Price Sync (§6.4, SYNC-1..6) — the ongoing-maintenance feature**, distinct from Stage 1 (one-off raw mirror) and Stage 2 (one-off promote): live cost-price propagation into already-promoted Packages, deactivation detection, and MYR currency conversion for non-MYR suppliers. This is now a confirmed, three-part design, not just "sync more often."
- **Price Sync Stage 3** (once there's a second supplier or a genuine same-supplier near-duplicate to test against): the cheapest-by-name query logic itself, in whatever serves the storefront/`CheckoutController` package listing — normalize the name (trim/lowercase/collapse whitespace) before grouping, deliberately hardening past the legacy system's raw-string-match fragility.
- Admin — Games & Packages (GAME-1..5/7) remaining pieces (GAME-6 drag-drop reorder, GAME-9/10 redundant with Product Manager, GAME-11 bulk price update, GAME-12/SEO fields) stay lower priority, not blocking.

**Live end-to-end checkout test + Payment Methods tool — design discussion, 2026-07-25:** founder asked to actually prove the checkout→payment→fulfillment pipeline works for real (not just automated tests) before building the Storefront. Attempted it directly against real Gamevion + Xendit sandbox credentials. Findings, in the order they were discovered:

1. **Gamevion sandbox mode is real, documented, and already correctly wired in `GamevionAdapter` — no code gap.** Confirmed directly from `docs.gamevion.com/gamevion-api` (fetched live): *"You can simulate transactions using sandbox mode. Use either: `X-ENVIRONMENT: sandbox` header - or query param `?mode=sandbox`. No saldo will be deducted in sandbox mode."* This applies to transactions generally, not just product listing. Re-checked `GamevionAdapter::client()` — it already sends `X-ENVIRONMENT: sandbox` on **every** request (including `createOrder()`) whenever the adapter is constructed with `sandbox: true`; an earlier claim in this same conversation that `createOrder()` doesn't respect sandbox mode was wrong and is corrected here. The only reason live orders would happen is `GAMEVION_SANDBOX=false` in `.env` — an environment setting, not a code fix needed.
2. **`POST /api/checkout` was exercised for real** (via `php artisan serve` + curl, with `GAMEVION_SANDBOX=true` passed as a one-off process env var — `.env` itself was never read or modified, Bash is blocked from touching it directly). Confirmed: Order creation, safe-failure behavior (Order stays Pending with null `payment_ref` on gateway failure — exactly as `CheckoutServiceTest` already proves), and real network round-trips to Xendit's actual sandbox API (`xnd_development_...` key) all work. Every attempt to actually complete a payment request failed on **Xendit-side channel configuration**, not our code:
   - `FPX_ABMB` — not even a real channel code (see point 3).
   - `DUITNOW_PAY` — rejected for missing `channel_properties.issuer_code`, which requires knowing valid issuer values from the Xendit Dashboard.
   - `DUITNOW_QR` — rejected with `INVALID_MERCHANT_SETTINGS` (channel not enabled/configured for this merchant account; DuitNow doesn't even appear in Xendit's own published MY channel list, see point 3 — likely a separately-enabled product).
   - Test orders created during this were cleaned up afterward (`KRS-01KYBT...` rows deleted); the `Reseller` "Platform Owner" seed row was kept (real seed data, not test pollution).
3. **Xendit has no API to discover which channels are enabled for a merchant account, or to query fee rates — confirmed against docs.xendit.co.** This differs fundamentally from Gamevion (which has a real `listProducts()` catalog to sync against) — so the Product-Manager-style "sync from supplier" pattern doesn't transfer to payment channels. Xendit **does** publish a static global reference list of valid channel-code *values* per country (fetched live, MY list: ~38 FPX bank codes like `AMBANK_FPX`/`MAYB2U_FPX`/`CIMB_FPX`, e-wallets `GRABPAY`/`SHOPEEPAY`/`TOUCHNGO`/`WECHATPAY`, `CARDS`, one virtual-account channel — DuitNow notably absent from this list) — so channel codes aren't guessed blindly, only *activation status per account* is unknowable via API.

**Resulting decision — Payment Methods admin tool** (SET-7/SET-11), founder chose **`/middleware`**, not `/admin/settings` (my recommendation, for consistency with where PRD already scopes SET-7/SET-11 — founder preferred grouping it with other external-integration-health tooling instead; noted as a deliberate deviation, not an oversight):

- **New `payment_methods` table** (migration `2026_07_25_160000_create_payment_methods_table.php`, model `app/Models/PaymentMethod.php` — **both created, migration not yet run, nothing else wired to them yet**): `channel_code` (unique, Xendit's exact value), `label`, `category` (UI grouping only — fpx/ewallet/card/etc — fee lookup is per-`channel_code` now, not per-category), `is_active` (default false), `percentage_rate`, `flat_fee_sen`, `requires_issuer` (bool, e.g. DuitNow Pay), `last_tested_at`, `last_test_result`. Replaces `config/checkout.php` entirely (to be deleted once the table is wired).
- **Seeder**: populate from the real MY channel-code list captured in point 3 above, all `is_active = false` — admin confirms each one via their own Dashboard or the "Test This Channel" action before flipping it on.
- **Simplification unlocked**: since every channel now has its own stored fee rate, `CreateCheckoutRequest`'s separate `payment_method` field (the old category-based key) becomes redundant — checkout should validate against `channel_code` alone (must exist + `is_active=true` in `payment_methods`), and derive `Order.payment_method` (the reporting label) from the matched row's `category`. Not yet implemented.
- **"Test This Channel" action**: fire a real (harmless) Xendit payment request for that specific channel, capture success/the exact error message into `last_test_result`/`last_tested_at` — directly reuses what was just done manually via curl above, as a proper admin tool. Same underlying idea as the separately-discussed Middleware Test Order tool below (DEV-1's dry-run concept), applied to payment channels instead of supplier orders.

**Remaining task list for next session** (in order):
1. Run the migration; write `PaymentMethodSeeder` with the real channel list; register it.
2. `Admin\PaymentMethodController` (or under `Middleware\`) — `index`, `updateStatus` (activate/deactivate), `updateFee`, `test`. Routes under `admin.role:super_admin,admin`.
3. Rewire `CheckoutController`/`CreateCheckoutRequest`/`PaymentMethodFeeResolver` to read from `PaymentMethod` (by `channel_code`) instead of `config('checkout.payment_methods')`; update `CheckoutControllerTest`'s payloads accordingly (currently use `payment_method` + `channel_code` as two separate fields — needs a fixture `PaymentMethod` row per test).
4. Delete `config/checkout.php` once nothing references it.
5. `/middleware/payment-methods` frontend screen — list (grouped by `category`), inline active toggle (mirror `/admin/games`'s status toggle pattern), fee edit, "Test" button showing `last_test_result`.
6. Tests for all of the above; update this §14/§15 tracker once done.

**Middleware Test Order tool — design discussion, 2026-07-25:** founder question after `/admin/orders` shipped: "so kita dah ada full workflow untuk test order?" — led to also wanting a way to test the Gamevion integration directly (game/package/player selectors, dry-run), referencing the legacy `manage.keroxshop.com/orders` screen. Two things surfaced in that discussion, both worth recording since neither is obvious from re-reading the legacy screenshot alone:
1. **Gamevion has a sandbox; a future supplier might not.** A test-order tool that assumes every supplier can be safely "dry run" against a real endpoint doesn't generalize. Resolution: a per-supplier `supports_sandbox` capability flag (not built yet), mirroring GAME-12's existing per-mapping `supports_validation` pattern — same shape of problem, same shape of fix.
2. **The founder independently caught that the legacy screenshot's bottom "Test Orders" table isn't actually test orders** — it's real and test order attempts mixed together (visible from the mix of `success`/`processing`/`refunded` statuses and real-looking player IDs), with no field distinguishing them. This is the same "two systems drift apart" failure class `legacy-reference-notes.md` already flagged elsewhere, one layer down. Resolution: a test-order attempt must never become an `Order` row at all — it calls `SupplierAdapter::createOrder()` directly, and its outcome belongs in Request Logs (MID-10/11), not a bespoke table, with an explicit `triggered_by` field so production and test calls share one auditable log without ever being ambiguous about which is which. This directly matches the PRD's own pre-existing "Dry Run" glossary line (§13) — "without creating real orders" — which the legacy system's own screen violates.

Neither piece is built yet — recorded here so the next session doesn't have to re-derive this reasoning from scratch, per this section's own stated purpose.

**Doc-consistency audit, 2026-07-25 (end of session):** founder asked for a full pass over today's code + docs to catch contradictions before committing. Found and fixed: **(1)** this very NEXT SESSION list still said the Games & Packages admin screen was unbuilt, after it had just been built in the same session — stale pointer, corrected above. **(2)** §8's `Package` data model row and GAME-7's requirement text still described `reseller_cost_price` as a directly admin-managed value, contradicting the same-session markup-% redesign — both updated to describe `markup_percent` as the real input. **(3)** ADR-005 got an addendum recording the live re-verification of Gamevion's API surface (done this session, for the input-fields/validation question) rather than leaving that research undocumented outside the chat. Routes-to-controllers cross-checked via `php artisan route:list` (all 30 API routes resolve to real methods, no dangling references) and the full test suite + `npm run build` re-run clean immediately before this note. No other contradictions found across `prd.md`/`adr.md`/`foundation-security.md`/`legacy-reference-notes.md`.

**Full codebase audit + bugfix pass, 2026-07-25 (new session, per the NEXT SESSION pointer above):** founder-requested audit of the whole backend + admin frontend for internal consistency and real bugs, done via four independent parallel reviews (money-core services, payment/supplier adapter layer, controllers/requests/migrations vs. §8's data model, admin frontend) each verifying the specific claims this document makes against the actual code, not just re-reading the prose. Result: **no PRD/ADR/docs-vs-code contradictions found anywhere** — every "verified"/"proven" claim in this section held up independently. Found and fixed 4 real issues:
- **[HIGH] `WithdrawalController::approve()` had no row lock** — two admins approving the same `Withdrawal` at nearly the same time could both pass the maker-checker check and both write a ledger debit for one request (the exact bug class already fixed in `OrderFulfillmentService::fulfill()` during the 2026-07-24 security review, but never applied here). Fixed the same way: `DB::transaction()` + `lockForUpdate()` on the `Withdrawal` row, re-checking status inside the lock. Proven with a new real two-OS-process test, `tests/Concurrency/WithdrawalApproveConcurrencyTest.php` (backed by `app:withdrawal-test-approve`), same pattern as `LedgerWithdrawConcurrencyTest`/`OrderFulfillmentConcurrencyTest` — concurrency suite is now 4 tests, not 3.
- **[MEDIUM] `VoucherController::issue()` wrote the `Voucher` row and its ledger debit as two separate, non-transactional writes** — a crash between them would leave a voucher with no matching ledger entry, quietly breaking ADR-002's "ledger is the sole source of truth" guarantee for this one path. Wrapped in `DB::transaction()`. While in the same file, also closed a related, previously-unflagged gap: `storeFromOrder()`'s "one voucher per order" check (PRD §7.5) was check-then-insert with no DB-level guarantee — added a unique index on `vouchers.order_id` (migration `2026_07_25_140000`, MySQL/SQLite both treat multiple NULLs as distinct so standalone Path A vouchers are unaffected) and a friendly `ValidationException` if a race loses against it.
- **[MEDIUM] `GamevionAdapter`'s HTTP client had no explicit timeout** — `createOrder()` runs inside `OrderFulfillmentService::fulfill()`'s row lock (the fix from the 2026-07-24 review), so a hung/slow Gamevion response could hold that lock (and its transaction) open indefinitely, risking lock-wait timeouts under load. Added `GAMEVION_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`/`GAMEVION_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (default 10s/5s), wired through `config/services.php` → `AppServiceProvider` → the adapter's `client()`.
- **[MEDIUM] Root-caused the known `UserDropdown.tsx` hydration mismatch** (flagged but not investigated in the 2026-07-25 entry above): `getClientSession()` was called directly in the render body, so SSR (no `sessionStorage`) rendered `"?"/"Account"` while the client's first render showed the real name — React saw a text mismatch. Fixed by moving the read into `useEffect` + `useState`, matching the pattern `admin/page.tsx` already used correctly. The same render-body-read pattern existed, latent, in 5 other screens (`product-manager`, `admin/users`, `admin/withdrawals`, `admin/vouchers`, `admin/games`) — none had visibly triggered the bug yet, but all five were standardized onto the effect-based pattern to close the gap before it did.

Also noted, not fixed (judged low-severity, left for a future pass): a Super Admin can self-demote their own `role` via the generic `PUT /admin-users/{id}` endpoint (only `updateStatus` has a self-change guard); `XenditGateway::mapStatus()` maps a failed `payment.capture` event to `Pending` rather than `Failed`; `GamevionAdapter::failureFrom()`'s `error` field check is untested against a non-boolean truthy value. Full test suite (167 sqlite + 4 concurrency) and `npm run build` re-run clean after all fixes.

**Player-ID Validation (MLBB) — design session + build, 2026-07-25 (new session):** founder asked to revisit "validate player ID" from scratch, starting with docs review (confirmed ADR-005's existing decision: validate-before-payment where supported, payment-then-validate-at-order-time fallback where not — and that Gamevion supports neither). Founder then supplied original research: three **unofficial, reverse-engineered** MLBB validate-ID endpoints from existing competitor top-up storefronts (AcidGameShop, Nexone.ph, MooGold) — not Gamevion, not any of our own suppliers. Before any design or code, live-tested all three (valid and invalid cases) against a real MLBB Malaysia account (`51049607`/`2005`) via direct `curl` calls — full findings in [ADR-005's newest addendum](./adr.md): AcidGameShop is cleanest (stateless JSON, `400` on invalid); Nexone needs a two-step token/cookie handshake; MooGold's `icon` field lies about validity (`status` is the real flag) and its `message` free-text shape isn't even internally consistent call-to-call.

Design discussion (talked through before writing code, per the founder's explicit request): fallback chain priority (Acid → Nexone → MooGold), how country gets normalized across three different response shapes (flag-emoji decode → ISO alpha-2, landed on as the one consistently-available signal), what happens when country can't be determined at all (`region_unknown` — block checkout, direct customer to contact support — same treatment as all-three-providers-down), and how a validated account's country maps to which of our own Game rows it must buy from (MUI-5, admin-curated — no provider API can supply this since none of them know our catalog).

**Built**: the full `PlayerValidator` chain + registry (`app/Services/PlayerValidation/`), schema (`player_validator_profiles`, `player_region_mappings`, `player_validations`), and the middleware Validators screen (MUI-5) — see the Player-ID Validation row in the table above for the complete file list. **Founder-caught correction mid-session**: the first cut modeled a validator as a raw string on `Game`, which the founder immediately flagged as unverifiable — "macam mana kita nak pastikan yang game X tu mmg ada button validator, dan adakah validator tu kita dah plug in ke belum dkt backend?" Restructured to a first-class `PlayerValidatorProfile` entity with a constrained key picker and a live "Test this validator" action — see ADR-005's addendum for the full reasoning.

**Bug found via real usage, not testing**: founder actually clicked "Run Test" in the browser and hit `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'map')` — traced to `PlayerValidatorProfileController::test()` returning the validator profile without its `mappings` relation eager-loaded (an unloaded Eloquent relation simply omits the key on serialization, it doesn't come back as `[]`), which the frontend's optimistic state-merge then treated as "this validator now has no mappings." Fixed on both sides: backend eager-loads `mappings.game` in all three `test()` response paths now (plus a regression test), frontend guards the render site with `?? []` so an incomplete API response can't crash the whole page again. All 49 tests for this feature pass; full suite verified clean (263 sqlite).

**Explicitly not built this session** (see NEXT SESSION below for the ordered follow-up): the `/admin/games` "Requires Player Validation" toggle (assign a profile + on/off — was deferred mid-session to focus on the Validators screen first), the storefront-facing "Validate Player ID" customer flow, `CheckoutController` enforcement via `player_validations` (schema exists, nothing writes to it yet), and MUI-7's distinct single-lookup admin tool (separate from MUI-5 — still a `null` nav placeholder — **MUI-7 later built 2026-08-28, merged onto MUI-5's own screen rather than standalone, see [ADR-052](./adr.md)**).

**24/7 burst-resilience baseline (ADR-014) — built, 2026-07-26.** While waiting on Gamevion support's reply to the sandbox `500` (ADR-006's newest addendum), founder asked to strengthen the foundation instead of idling — designed with the founder (grilled one decision at a time), then built same session. All six shipped: **(1) Queue** — `XenditWebhookController` dispatches `App\Jobs\FulfillOrderJob` (`database` driver) instead of calling `OrderFulfillmentService::fulfill()` inline; **(2) Indexing** — migration `2026_07_26_090000_add_resilience_indexes_to_orders_table.php` adds `orders.payment_ref` (every webhook's lookup column, was unindexed) and `(payment_status, delivery_status)` (Admin Orders filters); **(3) Retry** — `App\Services\Http\TransientFailureRetryPolicy` (shared by `GamevionAdapter`/`XenditGateway`, 3 attempts/200ms-500ms-1s backoff, connection failures + real 5xx only, never a 4xx) plus `FulfillOrderJob`'s own `$tries=3`/`backoff()`, plus **ORD-7**'s first real action — `OrderController::retryDelivery()` (`POST /api/orders/{order}/retry-delivery`) and a "Retry Delivery" button on `/admin/orders`'s detail view, both gated to `delivery_status=failed` only; **(4) Rate limit + health** — `throttle:10,1` on `POST /api/checkout`, new `GET /api/health` (DB + queue connectivity, unauthenticated); **(5) Caching** — `GameController::index()`/`packages()` and `PaymentMethodController::index()` cache their *unfiltered* listing only (60s TTL), invalidated immediately by every write that changes what they return (Game/Package update/markup/status/destroy, `SupplierProductController::promote()`, Payment Method status/fee/test) — a filtered/searched request always bypasses the cache; pricing/checkout (`CheckoutController`, `PaymentMethodFeeResolver`) read `Package`/`PaymentMethod` live, untouched; **(6) Observability** — `Log::withContext(['order_number' => ...])` set independently in the webhook controller and in `FulfillOrderJob` (separate processes, same key, grep-correlatable), `OrderFulfillmentService` extends it with `reference_number` once resolved and now logs a warning on delivery failure (previously silent — a business-level failure never throws). Founder explicitly declined Sentry/external error-tracking for now (file log only). Full suite: 283 passing (sqlite) + 4 concurrency (MySQL, unchanged this session) — up from 263 at the last checkpoint, entirely new coverage for this feature (retry-fires-then-succeeds, retry-skips-a-4xx, cache-hit-then-invalidates, rate-limit-429-on-11th-request, health-check, log-on-failure).

**Storefront session, 2026-07-26 (new session):** founder scoped 4 mandatory MVP pages up front (Homepage, per-game Top-Up page, Track Order, Order Review/Status), with a 5th (standalone public "Validate Player ID" lookup) explicitly marked optional/deferred. Built in this order, each discussed before coding, per the founder's own explicit process requirement:

1. **Homepage.** Founder supplied a real HTML draft (Figma-derived) plus real brand assets (logo emblem, banner) with founder-confirmed hex values. Critiqued against the `ui-ux-pro-max` skill's checklist before touching code — found the draft's `#10b981`-teal-on-navy palette and Manrope font were the two biggest "feels AI-generated" culprits (that exact combo is one of the most common defaults in AI-generated dark UIs), plus concrete bugs: "Log In" button and a bottom-nav "Account" item both contradict ADR-011 (guest-checkout, no Customer accounts), an icon-only search button under the 44px touch-target minimum, hotlinked Figma asset URLs that expire ~7 days, and a hero slider with no `prefers-reduced-motion` check. Rebuilt with the real brand tokens, Bebas Neue + Source Sans 3, Phosphor icons, and those bugs fixed — kept the section structure (already matched real top-up-storefront conventions), added one visually-featured product card instead of 8 identical ones. Promotions section kept as static/manual content for v1 (no Promotion model/admin screen exists — this reasoning applied to the Hero Banner backlog item too at the time this was written, but Hero Banner shipped 2026-07-26 and Promotions deliberately did not, per the founder's own distinction — see §14's Hero Banner row) — New Arrivals needs no new backend, sorts by `created_at`-equivalent on real data once wired.
2. **`/order/[slug]`.** First pass used a flat single-column form; founder caught that it didn't match a second set of 3 HTML references (top-up-page/order-review/order-status) supplied afterward, correctly identifying that skipping the gated-wizard rebuild (discussed but not actually implemented) — not placeholder-data timing — was the real cause. Rebuilt as a proper 3-step gated wizard, and **improvised, not copied**, to reconcile the founder's 3-page reference with a founder-approved 2-route simplification (Order Review became a modal, not a 3rd route, since `OrderForm` already holds all wizard state client-side — no need for the reference's URL-querystring page-to-page handoff): Step 1 branches on whether the game actually has a validator profile (real "Verify Account" vs. a plain "Continue" gate — the reference assumed every game supports verification, ours doesn't), contact details moved into the Review Modal (matching where the reference's "receipt" field lived, but made genuinely required instead of prefilled), Trust Strip dropped a fabricated "100K+ Successful Transactions" stat, and every input gained a real `<label>` (a real accessibility gap fixed in the same pass, not deferred).
3. **`/track-order` + `/order/status/[orderNumber]`.** Founder steer: Track Order should hand off into the same tracker used right after a real payment, not render its own separate result view — one component, two entry points. `/order/status/[orderNumber]` polls the new public endpoint every 5s while non-terminal.
4. **Order Review/Status page** — see point 2/3 above; consolidated into the Review Modal + shared status tracker rather than a distinct 4th route, a deliberate reduction from the founder's original page count.
5. **Validate Player ID standalone page** — deferred, per the founder's own original scoping (optional/"nanti").

**Price Sync — cost propagation + deactivation detection, design session, 2026-07-26 (new session):** founder question after reviewing `/middleware/product-manager` — is the price shown there real-time from Gamevion? Confirmed no: `Package.cost_price` is a one-time snapshot taken at promote time (already flagged and deferred on 2026-07-25, see this section's earlier entry and its own pointer at :608), and — a bigger surprise surfaced this session — **zero automation exists anywhere in the codebase**: `routes/console.php`/`bootstrap/app.php` register no `Schedule::` calls at all, so even Stage 1's raw-catalog mirror is 100% manual today. Rather than code straight into this, ran a full grilled design session (`mattpocock-skills`: grilling, codebase-design, domain-modeling) covering scope, trigger mechanism, deactivation semantics, price-propagation direction, audit logging, admin-vs-system reason tracking, UI placement, execution mode, and scheduler cadence — every decision recorded in **[ADR-015](./adr.md)**. Explicitly a plan-only session per the founder's own instruction — no code written this session. Key outcomes: two mechanisms scoped for the next build — **Price Propagation** (auto-applies supplier's price in both directions, no approval, every sync, logged to a new `price_change_logs` table) and **Deactivation Detection** (asymmetric: auto-off when the supplier deactivates or delists an item, but reactivation always goes through a new admin-reviewed **Pending Reactivation** queue — Approve or Dismiss, the latter converting `packages.deactivated_reason` from `supplier_sync` to `admin` so it stops being monitored). New `deactivated_reason`/`deactivated_at` columns on `packages`; new `price_sync_runs`/`price_change_logs` tables; a new async `SyncSupplierPricesJob` (admin-triggered now, scheduler-ready via `PRICE_SYNC_INTERVAL_MINUTES` for later); a new dedicated admin page `/middleware/price-sync` (deliberately not folded into Product Manager). Also surfaced, not this feature's problem to solve: the backend has **no production deployment at all** (still 100% local Laravel Herd, no VPS/cloud, no deploy config in the repo) — real scheduled automation (and reliable Xendit webhooks in production) both genuinely need that migration, tracked as its own separate initiative, not a blocker here.

**Price Sync Center — UI design session, 2026-07-26 (same session, immediately after ADR-015):** founder asked to bring the deferred "Center" UI design forward too, reasoning that a known data model is enough to design against even before it's built. Grilled against two real references — a "Sync Details" per-game modal mockup, and the actual live legacy `admin.keroxshop.com` Price Sync page (screenshot) — used strictly for layout/interaction inspiration, never for its field semantics (several terms needed correcting against this project's own domain model; full reasoning in **[ADR-016](./adr.md)**). Outcome: full 5-section page design (stat cards, Last Sync Details, Pending Reactivation with bulk Approve All/Dismiss All, a new **Manually Dismissed Packages** browsable list, and a paginated Sync History table opening a per-game Sync Details modal), plus one schema addition beyond ADR-015 (`price_sync_runs.triggered_by`). Currency Rate (SYNC-3) reconfirmed omitted — the legacy reference proves it's a real, solvable need for a future non-MYR supplier, but nothing to build against today. **ADR-016 is explicitly downstream of ADR-015 and expected to change if anything in ADR-015 changes during real implementation** — not a frozen contract. Still plan-only — no code written.

~~**➡️ NEXT SESSION STARTS HERE (2026-07-26 pointer, Price Sync design session).** Founder-confirmed next priority: **build ADR-015**...~~ **Done, 2026-07-26 (same day, build session) — see the shipped addendum below and [ADR-015's own shipped addendum](./adr.md).**

**Price Sync — Price Propagation + Deactivation Detection, built, 2026-07-26 (build session, same day as the design session above):** Price Propagation, Deactivation Detection, and the Pending Reactivation queue (SYNC-5/6) are now real code, not just design — 22 new tests added, full suite (350 tests) green. Ships exactly ADR-015's decision #1 scope: `packages.deactivated_reason`/`deactivated_at`, `price_sync_runs`/`price_change_logs` tables, `PackagePriceSyncService` (runs immediately after `ProductSyncService::sync()`'s own Stage 1 — no stage number of its own, since "Stage 2"/"Stage 3" already mean promote-to-catalog and the deferred cheapest-by-name resolution elsewhere in this doc), `SyncSupplierPricesJob` (mirrors `FulfillOrderJob`'s queued-job shape), the `Schedule::call()` entry (`PRICE_SYNC_INTERVAL_MINUTES`, still inert — no cron infra exists yet), and a **minimal** `/middleware/price-sync` page: "Sync All Prices Now" trigger + run-status polling + the Pending Reactivation table (single and bulk Approve/Dismiss). One real bug caught by a feature test before it could reach production: `SupplierProduct.last_synced_at` is a plain `datetime` column that truncates microseconds on write, so comparing it against the in-memory sync timestamp via `Carbon::equalTo()` always returned false — would have deactivated every promoted package on every real sync run. Fixed by comparing whole-second Unix timestamps instead; full story in ADR-015's shipped addendum. **Not built** (deliberately ADR-016's own territory, not ADR-015's): the stat cards, Sync History browsing, Sync Details modal, and Manually Dismissed Packages section — those need their own build pass.

~~Still open, not picked as next priority: the full Price Sync Center UI (ADR-016)...~~ **Done, 2026-07-27 (new session) — see the shipped addendum below and [ADR-016's own shipped addendum](./adr.md).**

**Price Sync Center — full UI shipped, 2026-07-27 (build session):** the full 5-section admin page ADR-016 designed is now real code: stat cards, Last Sync Details, Pending Reactivation (unchanged from ADR-015), **Manually Dismissed Packages** (new, paginated, Restore action), and a paginated **Sync History** table opening a per-game **Sync Details modal**. New backend: `PriceSyncController::index/stats/details`, `DismissedPackageController`, `PendingReactivationFinder` (extracted so the stat card's pending count and the queue itself share one query). One real gap found on contact with the data, not anticipated by either ADR: Deactivation Detection had no per-run audit trail at all — only `price_change_logs` existed, so a past run's Sync Details modal could show price diffs but not which game a deactivation belonged to. Fixed with a new `deactivation_logs` table, same shape as `price_change_logs`; full reasoning in [ADR-016's shipped addendum](./adr.md). Verified live in a real browser (trigger a run, stat cards update, Sync Details modal groups price/cost diffs and deactivations by game correctly, Restore reactivates regardless of live supplier status), not just `phpunit`. Full suite: 361 tests, all green (10 new).

Still open, not picked as next priority: Image Gallery (IMG-1/IMG-2, previous pointer above), ~~`CheckoutController` server-side `player_validations` enforcement (Player-ID Validation dependency-list item 3)~~ (**shipped 2026-07-27, see the Storefront row in §15 and ADR-005's newest addendum**), ~~MUI-7's distinct single-lookup admin tool~~ (**built 2026-08-28, see [ADR-052](./adr.md)**), Gamevion's sandbox `500` (ADR-006 — nothing to do but wait/follow up), VPS/cloud migration (ADR-015 addendum — no code blocker today, but the real reason automation and production webhooks can't fully land yet).

**Founder live-testing pass on the shipped Price Sync Center, 2026-07-27 (same day, real Herd environment, not the build session's own browser check):** founder triggered "Sync All Prices Now" for real against `kedairuncit-backend.test` (Laravel Herd) and reported the button stuck on "Syncing…" with no data ever updating. Root cause confirmed by inspecting the dev `jobs` table directly: **no queue worker was running** — Herd serves HTTP but does not run `php artisan queue:listen`/`queue:work` on its own (that's `composer run dev`'s job, alongside `serve`/`pail`/`npm run dev`; a Herd-served site still needs the worker started separately, e.g. in its own terminal, or via Herd Pro's per-site Queue service). Three stuck jobs (including one ancient orphaned one referencing an already-deleted run, unrelated cruft) were manually drained with `php artisan queue:work --stop-when-empty` to confirm the fix.

Draining that queue surfaced a second, more important real finding: this environment runs `GAMEVION_SANDBOX=true`, and Gamevion's sandbox catalog endpoint returns exactly **one hardcoded simulation product** (`external_ref: 234`, `mlbb5` — the same "Products Simulation (Sandbox)" item ADR-006's addendum already documented for order creation). Nobody had previously connected that limitation to **Price Sync's Deactivation Detection**: because the sandbox catalog never contains any real `supplier_package_ref`, every real promoted Package looks "vanished from the latest sync" to `PackagePriceSyncService`, and gets auto-deactivated — a false positive, not a real supplier-side change. This run did exactly that to 3 real packages (100 Diamonds, 210 Diamonds, 13 + 1 Diamonds), confirmed by their `deactivated_at` all matching the run's `finished_at` to the second. Manually reactivated all 3 immediately (root cause understood and confirmed false, so skipped the Pending Reactivation review step deliberately) — full reasoning recorded as a new addendum on **[ADR-006](./adr.md)**, since the limitation is Gamevion's sandbox environment, not Price Sync's own logic.

**Addendum — refined root cause, 2026-07-28 (client-demo prep session):** the gotcha above ("Herd doesn't run a worker on its own") is real but incomplete. Verified live while building a combined dev-startup script (`scripts/dev.sh`, this session): even when `composer run dev` *is* used — the documented fix — it can still silently drop `queue:listen` if `backend/`'s own `node_modules` was never installed (distinct from `admin/`/`storefront/`'s own installs). `npm run dev`'s `vite` step then fails immediately with `vite: command not found`, and `concurrently`'s `--kill-others` flag (part of the `dev` composer script itself) tears down `queue:listen`/`pail`/`php artisan serve` right along with it. Nothing in the admin/storefront terminals shows any sign of this — it's only visible in the backend's own terminal output, which is exactly why it went unnoticed until this session and can look identical to the 2026-07-27 symptom above (stuck job, no error surfaced anywhere the founder would normally be looking). Confirmed by re-running `composer run dev` fresh and reading its full output rather than assuming the fix from the prior entry was sufficient; fixed on this machine with `npm install` inside `backend/`. Recorded as an addendum rather than editing the entry above, per this doc's own no-silent-rewrite convention.

**Practical rule going forward:** don't trigger "Sync All Prices Now" for real while `GAMEVION_SANDBOX=true` — it will deactivate every real promoted Package, every time, until either a real (non-simulation) supplier catalog is reachable or Price Sync gains sandbox-awareness of its own (not built, not scoped yet).

**Order Resend Delivery (ADR-017) — shipped, 2026-07-27 (build session):** the richer, package-swap counterpart to ORD-7's plain `retryDelivery()`, designed the same day (design-only) and built immediately after. New `order_resend_attempts` table + `OrderResendAttempt` model (insert-audit, same discipline as `price_change_logs`/`deactivation_logs`). New `App\Services\Fulfillment\OrderResendService` enforces same-game-only package swap (decision #1), the order's own `delivery_status=failed` guard (unchanged from `retryDelivery()`), and — only when the game has player validation enabled — a recent `player_validations` row before the resend is allowed (decision #6, reusing `CheckoutController`'s own data-tuple-match check). `platform_profit`/`reseller_profit` are recomputed via the existing `PricingService::calculate()` against the target package's *live* cost values and set on `Order` before `OrderFulfillmentService::fulfill()` runs, so its existing `creditProfit()` (fires exactly once, on success) credits the ledger with the resend's real economics — `cost_price`/`reseller_cost_price`/`selling_price`/`final_amount`/`transaction_fee` are never touched (decision #2), matching the checkout-time flow's own existing "stored profit is an estimate until delivery actually succeeds" behavior, not a new invariant. New `OrderController::resend()` (`POST /api/orders/{order}/resend`) pre-validates synchronously for fast admin feedback, then dispatches `ResendOrderDeliveryJob` (ADR-014 async discipline — never block the admin panel on a live Gamevion call); the job re-derives live package values at actual attempt time. Admin UI: `/admin/orders`' existing detail view gained a single **"Resend Delivery…"** button (`delivery_status=failed` gate) opening `ResendDeliveryModal` — same-game active-package picker **defaulted to the order's own package** (a founder-requested merge, same session: this one flow now covers both "just retry the same package" and "swap to a different package," instead of two separate buttons; the submit button itself relabels "Retry Delivery"/"Resend Delivery" depending on whether the selection changed), a live cost-price reconciliation preview ("Platform absorbs ±RM x.xx"), an inline "Verify Player ID" step that only renders when the game requires it, and an optional note — plus a new **Delivery Logs** table rendering every `order_resend_attempts` row, most recent first. ORD-7's original plain `POST /api/orders/{order}/retry-delivery` endpoint (ADR-014) is unchanged on the backend, just no longer called from this UI. 21 new backend tests (`OrderResendServiceTest` 12, `ResendOrderDeliveryJobTest` 4, `OrderControllerTest` +5); full suite 396, all green (361 at the last checkpoint + 8 Image Gallery + 6 checkout-enforcement tests already shipped earlier this session + this session's own 21). `npx tsc --noEmit`/`next build`/`eslint` all clean. Verified live against a real running `php artisan serve` + real Sanctum token (cross-game package correctly 422s, same-game package correctly 200s and queues the job) — **not** verified live end-to-end through an actual queued Gamevion call, deliberately, since that risks either reproducing ADR-006's sandbox `500` or a real production call depending on `GAMEVION_SANDBOX`, neither of which should happen as a side effect of testing this feature. Decision #9 (cross-game package swap) remains explicitly deferred, not built — see [ADR-017's shipped addendum](./adr.md) for the full reasoning.

**Second-pass resilience & security audit + docs restructure — 2026-07-27 (design-only session):** ran a full read-only re-audit (4 scoped reviews: DB indexing/caching, queue/retry/load-balancing, observability, OWASP-adjacent security) to verify ADR-014's six decisions against the actual code rather than trust the design doc, and to check the security surface ADR-014 never scoped. Result, recorded in **[ADR-019](./adr.md)**: all six of ADR-014's decisions are genuinely shipped as designed; the two things that looked like gaps (no Redis, no Sentry) are the founder's own already-recorded deferrals, not oversights. Real new findings, sequenced by deploy-dependency rather than raw severity — fix-now items (no production-host dependency): missing `orders.created_at` index + a `whereDate()` query that defeats indexing regardless, unverified FK indexes on 7 tables, no idempotency key on Xendit payment creation, no explicit HTTP timeout on `XenditGateway` (unlike `GamevionAdapter`), silent checkout-failure and login-attempt logging, no rate limit on `/api/login`, MFA columns that exist but are never checked, and the Image Gallery upload disk being hardcoded rather than config-driven (corrects this doc's own §15 Image Gallery note — see that row). Deferred-until-production-host items: a real queue-worker process (`docker-compose.yml` still defines MySQL only), Redis for cache/queue, S3 for uploads, centralized log shipping. Deferred-until-real-traffic items: request correlation IDs, queue/error-rate metrics, scheduling the existing smoke tests as synthetic monitoring, client-side error tracking in `admin/`/`storefront`. No code changed this session — design/decision only, per this doc's own established practice for this kind of finding (ADR-013/015/016/017/018). **Also this session:** root `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` (previously generic, unused multi-agent-tooling boilerplate) replaced with project-specific orientation; new `backend/CLAUDE.md` and root `README.md` added — see ADR-019 decision #4.

**Production host chosen — ADR-020, design session, 2026-07-29:** the production-infra gap ADR-015/016/019 kept flagging as "genuinely blocked, no host chosen" is now resolved on paper. Founder had already researched a concrete shape (DigitalOcean pricing calculator, a hand-drawn single-droplet architecture diagram) before this session; grilled one decision at a time (`/mattpocock-skills:grilling`), same discipline as ADR-014 through ADR-019. Governing priority, stated explicitly by the founder and re-applied to every sub-decision: cost is *not* the optimization target — reliability/no-crash, maintainability, and scalability toward an eventual expand-beyond-Malaysia plan matter more, and several decisions below deliberately spend more than the founder's own initial research proposed as a result. Full decision set recorded in **[ADR-020](./adr.md)**:
- **Compute:** one DigitalOcean Basic Droplet (8 GiB/4 vCPU/160 GiB SSD, Singapore region) + DigitalOcean Droplet Backups add-on. One droplet/one region for now — Load Balancer + a second droplet/region deferred until a real traffic/launch-date signal exists, not provisioned speculatively.
- **Database:** moved *off* the droplet to DigitalOcean **Managed MySQL** (reversing the founder's own initial single-box research) — money-critical `orders`/`ledger_entries`/`withdrawals`/`vouchers` data shouldn't share a fault domain with the app processes, and Managed MySQL's optional standby-node failover closes the "droplet crash loses everything since the last backup" gap self-hosted MySQL can't.
- **Cache:** Redis, self-hosted in a Docker container, cache-only — **explicitly supersedes ADR-014 decision #1's Redis deferral** (recorded as a new entry per this doc's no-silent-rewrite convention, not an edit to that one). Queue stays on the `database` driver, now backed by Managed MySQL. Self-hosted (not Managed Redis/Valkey) is deliberately correct here since Redis only ever holds regenerable cache — three explicit revisit triggers recorded in the ADR if that stops being true.
- **Containerization:** Docker Compose in production (does not reopen `ADR-010`, which was scoped to local dev only), chosen for scale/maintainability over minimizing today's RAM footprint.
- **Process supervision:** Docker-native restart policies + Laravel's own `--max-jobs`/`--max-time` flags, no Supervisor — queue workers split into separate services by queue name (`orders` vs `price-sync`) so a slow Price Sync run can never block urgent order fulfillment.
- **Network/security:** DigitalOcean Cloud Firewall (80/443/22 only), SSH key-only + fail2ban. DNS stays at Cloudflare (founder's existing registrar) with Cloudflare Proxy enabled (DDoS protection + edge CDN — directly useful for the international-expansion plan) and a Cloudflare Origin CA certificate (15-year validity, no renewal cron) instead of Let's Encrypt. A DigitalOcean Reserved IP is what Cloudflare's DNS actually points at.
- **CI/CD:** GitHub Actions, fully automated, no manual approval gate — backend fast + concurrency test suites, admin/storefront build/lint/typecheck, build 3 Docker images, push to DigitalOcean Container Registry, SSH deploy, post-deploy health-check with auto-rollback. Confirmed by inspection (not assumed) that this repo has zero CI/CD and zero E2E test infrastructure today. Playwright E2E for the golden paths (checkout, admin resend) is recorded as **decided but explicitly deferred** — the immediate next priority after this ADR, not required to gate the first deploy given the existing 396+ backend tests are already a strong gate.
- **Build sequencing:** Dockerfiles/`docker-compose.yml`/GitHub Actions (everything except the final SSH-deploy step) can be built and proven now, before the droplet is actually purchased — none of it depends on real infra existing yet.

**Explicitly not done this session** (design only, per founder's own instruction — implementation is a new session): nothing is purchased or provisioned — no droplet, no Managed MySQL instance, no Spaces buckets, no Reserved IP, no Cloudflare Origin cert, no Dockerfiles, no GitHub Actions workflow. This ADR is independent of, and doesn't change, ADR-019's own fix-now list (login rate-limiting, Xendit idempotency key, `SupplierCircuitBreaker`, etc.) — those remain separately tracked pure-code items with no infra dependency.

**ADR-019 fix-now list shipped, 2026-07-29 (same day, new session — a read-only project audit turned into an implementation pass at the founder's direction):** every Decision-1 item built and tested except MFA (explicitly descoped, founder's own call — see [ADR-019's newest addendum](./adr.md) for the full reasoning on each item, not duplicated here). Summary:
- `orders.created_at` index + `OrderController`'s `today` filter fixed (`whereDate()` → `whereBetween()`).
- FK-index audit against real MySQL found all 7 flagged tables already properly indexed — but surfaced a genuine, previously-unknown bug instead: two migrations' auto-generated compound-index names exceed MySQL's 64-char limit (`player_region_mappings`, `player_validations`), invisible on sqlite, both now fixed with explicit names. Worth a full migration sweep for the same pattern before the first real production deploy (ADR-020).
- `XenditGateway` HTTP timeout added (10s/5s, config-driven). Idempotency-key research done directly against docs.xendit.co: Xendit has no client idempotency-key header on Payment Request v3; its real dedupe is server-side `reference_id` uniqueness (409 on a genuine duplicate), which already protects same-Order retries. The real remaining gap — a retried `POST /api/checkout` minting a brand-new `order_number` each time — needs a client-supplied checkout-level idempotency key, a `storefront/` contract change not built this session.
- Checkout failures and admin login attempts (success + failure) now logged. `POST /api/login` rate-limited (`throttle:5,1`).
- Image Gallery disk is no longer hardcoded — new `filesystems.gallery_disk` config (`GALLERY_DISK` env, defaults `public`), deliberately **not** reusing the app-wide `FILESYSTEM_DISK` (already set to `local` in this env for unrelated reasons — reusing it would have silently broken gallery's public-URL requirement).
- **`SupplierCircuitBreaker` built** — `App\Services\CircuitBreaker\CircuitBreaker` (cache-backed, closed/open) wrapping `GamevionAdapter` via `CircuitBreakingSupplierAdapter`. Only trips on a genuine server error (`SupplierResponse::isServerError`, new field) — an ordinary 4xx business rejection resets the failure count instead of counting toward tripping, a correctness fix beyond this item's original one-line description. Wired into the real `SupplierAdapter` container binding, so `OrderFulfillmentService`'s actual delivery path is protected, not just a standalone class. `foundation-security.md` §6 checked off.
- Full suite green throughout: 416 fast-suite tests, 4 concurrency tests against real MySQL.

**Live-testing session, Gallery images → storefront/admin `next/image`, 2026-08-12:** founder uploaded a real image via `/admin/gallery` and pasted its URL into `EditGameModal`/`HeroSlideModal`'s `image_url` field (the paste-target workflow §15's Image Gallery row already documents) — surfaced two separate `next/image` config gaps, not one:
1. **`images.remotePatterns` unset** — `storefront/next.config.ts`/`admin/next.config.ts` had no `images` key at all, so `next/image` refused every external URL outright (`Invalid src prop … hostname "kedairuncit-backend.test" is not configured`). Fixed: both files now allow `kedairuncit-backend.test` (https) and `127.0.0.1:8000` (http) under `/storage/**` only, not a blanket wildcard.
2. **`dangerouslyAllowLocalIP` (Next 16 SSRF guard)** — fixing (1) only changed the failure mode, not the outcome: the image request still 400'd server-side with `"url" parameter is not allowed`, logged as `upstream image … resolved to private ip ["127.0.0.1"]`. Next.js's built-in image-optimizer refuses to fetch any upstream host that DNS-resolves to a private/loopback IP, regardless of `remotePatterns` — and Herd's `*.test` domains resolve straight to `127.0.0.1`. Fixed by setting `images.dangerouslyAllowLocalIP: process.env.NODE_ENV !== "production"` in both configs — deliberately dev-only, since a real production backend domain won't resolve to a private IP and shouldn't carry this bypass.

Both are local-dev-environment issues, not app bugs — confirmed the underlying image (`curl`/Node `fetch` with the same `NODE_OPTIONS=--use-system-ca` flag `storefront/package.json`'s `dev` script already sets) loaded fine outside Next's image optimizer the whole time. **Before the first production deploy, `remotePatterns` in both `next.config.ts` files must be updated to the real API domain** — the two entries added this session are dev-only hosts and won't match production's hostname, which would otherwise reproduce gap (1) again post-deploy for an unrelated reason. See §15's Storefront row.

**Same session, also:** `EditGameModal`'s and `HeroSlideModal`'s `image_url` field each got a recommended-dimension hint (800×500px/16:10 for game cards — matches `ProductCard`'s `aspect-16/10`; 1600×800px/2:1 for hero banners — matches `HeroSlider`'s ~1.97:1 desktop display ratio), derived from the actual rendered CSS rather than guessed.

**How to run tests:**
- Fast, everyday suite (sqlite, no Docker needed): `cd backend && php artisan test`
- Concurrency suite (needs the dockerized MySQL): `cd backend && docker compose up -d && php artisan test -c phpunit.concurrency.xml`

**Known gotchas to remember:**
- Tests that spawn a real subprocess to prove locking (the two `tests/Concurrency/*ConcurrencyTest.php` files) must use `DatabaseMigrations`, not `RefreshDatabase` — `RefreshDatabase` wraps a test in one uncommitted transaction that's rolled back at the end, so a spawned subprocess (a separate DB connection) would never see the seeded data.
- Any real end-to-end test of a queued job (Price Sync, order fulfillment) needs a queue worker actually running — `cd backend && composer run dev` (serve + `queue:listen` + pail + vite together), or at minimum `php artisan queue:listen --queue=orders,price-sync,backups --tries=1 --timeout=0` in its own terminal alongside Herd. Without it, dispatched jobs sit in the `jobs` table forever at `queued` — not a frontend bug, not something a browser refresh fixes. **Updated 2026-07-30 (ADR-020 Phase 1 addendum):** since `FulfillOrderJob`/`ResendOrderDeliveryJob`/`SyncSupplierPricesJob` now declare real queue names (`orders`/`price-sync`, no longer the implicit `default`), a bare `queue:listen`/`queue:work` with no `--queue=` flag processes **none** of them — `composer run dev`'s own `queue:listen` call was updated to `--queue=orders,price-sync` for exactly this reason; any other manual worker invocation must do the same or it will silently process nothing. **Further updated 2026-08-26 (ADR-039):** `RunDatabaseBackupJob` added a third real queue name, `backups` — `composer run dev`'s `queue:listen` and `docker-compose.prod.yml`'s worker set both updated to match; the `--queue=` flag shown here is now three names, not two.
- Don't trigger a real Price Sync run while `GAMEVION_SANDBOX=true` (see the founder live-testing pass above) — it will falsely deactivate every real promoted Package.

**Fat-controller audit follow-up, 2026-08-24 (new session) — `VoucherController::issue()` moved to `VoucherService`.** A read-only whole-codebase audit for fat controllers/sync issues/duplicate code (prompted by the founder ahead of the Digiflazz ADR work below) found one real gap: `VoucherController` held a private `issue()` method that wrote the `Voucher` row and debited the platform ledger directly inside a controller-owned `DB::transaction()`, the only money-write path in the codebase not living in a Service class (every sibling — `LedgerService::withdraw()`, `VoucherService::redeem()`/`restore()`/`commit()`, `OrderFulfillmentService::fulfill()` — already does). Fixed: `issue()` moved verbatim into `VoucherService` (now constructor-injects `LedgerService`), `VoucherController::store()`/`storeFromOrder()` call `$this->vouchers->issue(...)` instead; `generateCode()` moved with it. Five tests that constructed `new VoucherService()` directly (`FulfillOrderJobTest`, `ResendOrderDeliveryJobTest`, `OrderResendServiceTest`, `OrderFulfillmentServiceTest`, `CheckoutServiceTest`) updated to pass `new LedgerService()`.
