**Fresh full-system audit, 2026-08-14 (new session), per the founder's own standing instruction above** — three independently-scoped agents (no prior-audit context carried in, to avoid re-confirming old blind spots), covering backend money/security paths, the admin panel, and the storefront + both apps' `npm audit`/`composer audit`. Full findings not duplicated here; the money paths (checkout, ledger, withdrawal maker-checker, voucher redemption, both webhook controllers' signature+amount verification, circuit breaker) were all re-verified independently and hold up — no Critical found there. One genuine **Critical** found and fixed same session (below). Everything else the audit surfaced is folded into the updated pointer at the end of this entry.

**Critical fix shipped, same session — `/middleware/*` and `/blacklist` routes were reachable by a regular `admin` role, not just `super_admin`.** PRD §3 Users & Roles is explicit: "Admin ... Cannot modify system settings, supplier credentials, or blacklist rules." `admin/src/app/middleware/layout.tsx`'s own doc comment already flagged this whole area as "SUPER-ADMIN-ONLY... TODO: add a role check" — but `backend/routes/api.php` had every `/middleware/*` group (sandbox, supplier-products, price-sync, payment-methods, validators) and the `/blacklist` group gated `admin.role:super_admin,admin`, not `super_admin` alone, and the frontend TODO was never actioned. Net effect: any account provisioned with the plain `admin` role (not just `super_admin`) could activate/deactivate payment channels, edit supplier validator profiles, promote supplier products into real `Package`s, trigger price syncs, and add/deactivate blacklist entries — all explicitly reserved for `super_admin` by design. Fixed: all 6 route groups tightened to `admin.role:super_admin` in `backend/routes/api.php`; `admin/src/app/middleware/layout.tsx` converted to a client component with a real `getClientSession().role !== "super_admin"` redirect-to-`/admin` guard (mirrors `admin/users/page.tsx`'s existing session-check pattern), closing the TODO. 9 backend test files updated (`actingAsAdmin()` helpers switched from `role: 'admin'` to `role: 'super_admin'` since these controllers now genuinely require it, one `PaymentMethodCatalogControllerTest` case fixed the same way) plus a new `test_regular_admin_is_forbidden` case added to each of the 9 — proving the tightened gate, not just assuming it. Full suite **561/561 green**. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean on the touched `admin/` file.

~~**➡️ NEXT SESSION STARTS HERE (2026-08-14 pointer, updated after the fresh audit + critical-fix above).** ... 1. **Small, mechanical fixes, unordered among themselves:**...~~ Founder picked this batch to run next, same session — see below for what shipped and what's still genuinely blocked.

**Small mechanical fixes batch — shipped, 2026-08-14 (same session).** Six of the seven items closed; the two dead-link items turned out not to be mechanical at all (see below), left open rather than faked.

- **Webhook rate limiting**: both `/api/webhooks/xendit` and `/api/webhooks/chip` now carry `throttle:120,1` (`backend/routes/api.php`) — generous enough not to risk throttling a real gateway retry burst. Test: `XenditWebhookControllerTest::test_rate_limits_repeated_requests_from_the_same_ip`.
- **Sanctum token expiration**: `config/sanctum.php`'s `expiration` is now `env('SANCTUM_TOKEN_EXPIRATION_MINUTES', 720)` (12h, Sanctum's own rolling `created_at`-window enforcement — no `expires_at` column write needed at issuance). `admin/src/lib/api-client.ts`'s `apiFetch`/`apiUpload` gained a central `handleUnauthenticated()`: any `401` clears the client session and redirects to `/login`, so an expired token degrades cleanly instead of every page growing its own handling. Test: `AuthControllerTest::test_token_older_than_the_configured_expiration_is_rejected`.
- **Blacklist email case-sensitivity**: `BlacklistService::check()`'s email branch now compares `LOWER(value)` against `Str::lower($email)`, matching `VoucherService::assertUsable`'s existing convention for the same kind of match. Test: `BlacklistServiceTest::test_matches_a_blacklisted_email_case_insensitively`.
- **Voucher/withdrawal amount ceilings**: `CreateVoucherRequest` capped at `max:1000000` (RM 10,000), `CreateWithdrawalRequest` at `max:100000000` (RM 1,000,000) — same fat-finger-guard reasoning as `markup_percent`'s existing `max:1000`. Tests: `VoucherControllerTest::test_amount_above_the_sanity_ceiling_is_rejected_even_for_super_admin`, `WithdrawalControllerTest::test_amount_above_the_sanity_ceiling_is_rejected`.
- **Price Sync bulk-action busy state**: `admin/src/app/middleware/price-sync/page.tsx` gained a `bulkBusy` state disabling "Approve All"/"Dismiss All" during the in-flight request, matching every single-row action's existing `rowBusyId` pattern.
- **`next` dependency bump**: both `admin/` and `storefront/` moved `16.2.11` → `16.3.1`. `npm audit` is now **0 findings** in both apps (previously 3 high in each, `nanoid`/`postcss`/`sharp` via `next`). `tsc --noEmit`/`next build` clean on both.
- **Storefront dead links — not fixed, flagged as blocked instead of faked.** `SeoBlurb.tsx`'s "Read More ›" and `SiteFooter.tsx`'s 4 social icons are all `href="#"` because there is no real destination yet — no full SEO article page exists, no real social media accounts were provided. Fabricating a URL here would be worse than the placeholder; needs the founder to supply real destinations (or a decision to remove the affordance) before this can actually close.

Full backend suite **566/566 green** (up from 561). `composer.json`/`package.json` diffs are dependency-version-only, no lockfile drift beyond `next` and its own transitive tree.

~~**➡️ NEXT SESSION STARTS HERE (2026-08-14 pointer, updated after the mechanical-fixes batch above).** Remaining, sequenced easiest-to-fix first...~~ Founder picked item 2's ORD-10 next, same session — turned out to need its own design pass, not a plain "just build it" — see below.

**ORD-10 delivery-side reconciliation — grilled and recorded as ADR-026, 2026-08-14 (same session), design only, not yet built.** Founder asked to understand the problem before building — that conversation surfaced it's structurally harder than payment-side's own reconciliation (ADR-021), not just unbuilt out of neglect. Two distinct, previously-undocumented ambiguous-failure shapes exist: **Gap X** — a connection failure that exhausts every retry layer leaves an order silently stuck at `delivery_status=processing` forever, zero visibility, discoverable today only by querying `failed_jobs` directly; **Gap Y** — Gamevion's idempotent `409 duplicate_reference` response is already recorded in `supplier_response.error_code` by existing code but never acted on. Unlike payment-side, Gamevion's `checkStatus()` needs *its own* invoice number, which is exactly what's missing in both gaps — confirmed live this session (re-checked API docs, blocked by a `403` on the live re-fetch, so resting on the last-confirmed 2026-07-25 record; founder additionally reviewed Gamevion's real merchant dashboard live — no reference-number search exists there either, date-range + TARGET/SERVICE columns are the only usable cross-reference). Founder specifically asked about real-world trigger probability at volume (2,000 concurrent orders) — answered with structural reasoning (both retry layers already absorb most transient blips; burst-driven supplier-side slowness is the real volume-linked risk factor), not a fabricated percentage, since no live production data exists yet to measure it.

Full 6-decision design in **[ADR-026](./adr.md)**, not duplicated here — summary: new `DeliveryStatus::NeedsReview` enum case; new `app:reconcile-pending-deliveries` command (15-min cadence, mirrors `app:reconcile-pending-payments`) that auto-retries Gap X once (reusing the existing `reference_number`, Gamevion-confirmed idempotent) and routes any `duplicate_reference` outcome (Gap X's retry or pre-existing Gap Y) straight to `NeedsReview`; three exit actions — new "Mark as Delivered" (admin manually cross-references Gamevion's dashboard, submits the real invoice number found, which populates `supplier_ref` and credits ledger profit), "Resend Delivery" (existing flow, gate extended), and **"Issue Voucher" explicitly blocked** from this state — the one deliberate non-exit, since voucher issuance from an unconfirmed delivery is exactly the double-compensation risk (real game credits + a refund voucher) this feature exists to prevent. Admin UI reuses the existing `/admin/orders` list+detail pattern (new `needs_review` filter, no new screen). No backfill needed — the scheduled job picks up any pre-existing stuck order on its first run. Explicitly flagged as Gamevion-specific mechanics (the idempotent-retry assumption), not a `SupplierAdapter`-wide guarantee — re-verify before ever extending to a second supplier. Explicitly **not built this session** — implementation is next session's own work.

~~**➡️ NEXT SESSION STARTS HERE (2026-08-14 pointer, updated after ADR-026 was recorded).** Remaining... 1. **Build ADR-026**...~~ Founder said to build it through to completion, same session — see below.

**ADR-026 (ORD-10 delivery-side reconciliation) built, 2026-08-14 (same session), test-first.** No migration needed — `orders.delivery_status` is a plain string column (`database/migrations/2026_07_23_153930_create_orders_table.php`), never a DB-level enum, so the new `DeliveryStatus::NeedsReview` case (`app/Services/Order/DeliveryStatus.php`) needed no schema change. `OrderStatusService` gained `markNeedsReview()` (Processing or Failed → NeedsReview) and `markDeliveredManually()` (NeedsReview → Delivered only); `startDelivery()`'s allowed-current-states extended to include NeedsReview so Resend Delivery can re-enter Processing from it. `OrderFulfillmentService::fulfill()`'s failure branch now special-cases `error_code === 'duplicate_reference'` — routes to `NeedsReview` instead of `Failed`, universally (a first attempt, a manual resend, or the new command's own retry), which also retroactively fixes a manual "Resend Delivery" click hitting duplicate_reference — it now shows the correct ambiguous signal immediately instead of a misleading plain "failed." New public `markDeliveredManually(Order, string $supplierRef, ?string $note, string $confirmedBy)` on the same service — same lock/transaction discipline as `fulfill()`, credits ledger profit and commits any reserved voucher redemption exactly like a normal successful delivery, stores the admin's confirmation (who, when, note) into `supplier_response` since this order's outcome is human-confirmed, not gateway-confirmed.

New `App\Console\Commands\Fulfillment\ReconcilePendingDeliveriesCommand` (`app:reconcile-pending-deliveries`, scheduled every 15 min in `routes/console.php`, mirroring `app:reconcile-pending-payments`): Gap X (stuck `processing`, no `supplier_ref`, stale >15 min) re-dispatches `FulfillOrderJob` — never calls the supplier synchronously from the command itself (ADR-014); Gap Y (stale `Failed` rows already carrying `error_code=duplicate_reference` from before this shipped) gets a one-time, row-locked catch-up transition straight to `NeedsReview`. New config `services.delivery_reconciliation.stale_after_minutes` (default 15, env-overridable).

**A real bug found and fixed while building, not just at design time**: `OrderResendService::assertResendable()` — the job-level re-check `ResendOrderDeliveryJob` actually runs — still only allowed `Failed`, separate from `OrderController::resend()`'s own pre-check which I'd already extended to allow `NeedsReview`. Without this fix the admin-facing "Resend Delivery" button would have accepted the request while the queued job silently rejected it. Both guards now agree; a dedicated regression test (`OrderResendServiceTest::test_allows_resend_from_a_needs_review_order`) proves the job-level path specifically, not just the controller's own pre-check.

Admin: `OrderController` gained `markDelivered()` (`POST /api/orders/{order}/mark-delivered`, new `MarkOrderDeliveredRequest` requiring `supplier_ref`) plus a `needs_review` status filter, distinct from `need_action` — the whole point being "Issue Voucher" is deliberately **never** shown for this state (ADR-026 decision 4c is enforced automatically here: that action was already gated to `Failed` only and was never touched). `retryDelivery()`/`resend()` both extended to accept `NeedsReview` alongside `Failed`. Frontend: `admin/src/lib/orders.ts` gained `needs_review` to the `delivery_status`/`OrderStatusFilter` types and `markOrderDelivered()`; new `MarkDeliveredModal.tsx` (requires the real Gamevion invoice number, mirrors `IssueVoucherModal.tsx`'s pattern) and `NeedsReviewBanner.tsx` (surfaces `created_at`/`player_id`/game prominently — exactly what's needed to cross-reference Gamevion's dashboard, ADR-026 decision 5) on `/admin/orders`' existing detail view; `middleware/sandbox/page.tsx`'s own copy of the delivery-status color map updated to satisfy the shared type (sandbox orders structurally can't reach `needs_review`, TypeScript just needed the key).

27 new/changed backend tests (`OrderStatusServiceTest` +7, `OrderFulfillmentServiceTest` +5, `OrderResendServiceTest` +1, new `ReconcilePendingDeliveriesCommandTest` 7, `OrderControllerTest` +7); full backend suite **593/593 green** (up from 566). `npx tsc --noEmit`/`next build` clean on `admin/`; pre-existing `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint findings on touched files confirmed unrelated (same lines flagged before this session's changes, verified via `git stash`).

**ADR-026 sandbox coverage — same session, addendum.** Founder asked whether `needs_review` could be exercised in the ADR-018 sandbox too. Turned out mostly free: the sandbox's existing "Simulated Outcome" error-code field is free-text and already routes through the same `OrderFulfillmentService::fulfill()`, so typing `duplicate_reference` reaches `needs_review` with zero backend change. Two real gaps closed: `SandboxOrderController::markDelivered()` (reuses `markDeliveredManually()` unchanged — `is_test` already keeps ledger credits off), and `/middleware/sandbox`'s own Resend button was gated `failed`-only, same oversight the real page had before this ADR — extended to match, both frontend and the sandbox's own `assertResendable()`-equivalent (it delegates to the same shared `OrderResendService`, so the earlier job-level fix already covered it). Frontend: `MarkDeliveredModal`/`NeedsReviewBanner` both gained a `sandbox` prop (same pattern `ResendDeliveryModal` already established) with sandbox-appropriate copy. 7 new backend tests, full suite **598/598 green**. `tsc`/`next build` clean. Full detail in [ADR-026's newest addendum](./adr.md).

Phases 1-5 shipped (schema, `/admin/membership` config UI + pricing preview, public catalog member-price wiring, email OTP + session token, `/membership` storefront page) — see below. **Phase 6 (checkout wiring + race-safe quota decrement + cycle reset + OrderResendService member formula + admin order-detail member badge) shipped 2026-08-29 via PR #23; Phase 6.5 (member registry + fee collection + membership money views) shipped 2026-08-29 — see the two session-summary entries at the end of this section and [ADR-027's 2026-08-29 addendum](./adr.md).** Still open: Phase 7 (WhatsApp renewal reminder, vendor unpicked), plus the founder's own real fee/quota/discount% numbers (still seeded drafts) and Plunk — only the domain verification itself remains: the Plunk project + `send.fixfastapp.com` sender domain + DNS records were provisioned 2026-08-29 (`feature/plunk-sender-setup`, see the latest session summary at the end of this section); verification is `Pending` (SES pipeline lag, auto-verifies) and the flow is otherwise proven end-to-end via `tinker`-bypassed testing.

**Platform & Reseller Settings — grilled and recorded as ADR-028, 2026-08-21 (same session).** Surfaced while briefing the founder on the storefront dead-link/WhatsApp-number backlog items (items 1/2, standing pointer above) — the founder correctly reframed both as symptoms of a missing admin-editable settings mechanism (`prd.md` §6.16/§6.17, 100% unbuilt), not withheld content, and asked to grill the real fix instead of treating them as blocked. Full 9-decision design in [ADR-028](./adr.md), not duplicated here — summary: SET-1..11 split into `reseller_branding` (new table, 1:1 `Reseller` — store name/description/support contact/footer/social links/T&C content, since a future affiliate/reseller direction the founder confirmed is real, not hypothetical) and `platform_settings` (new singleton table — currency, bulk markup action, maintenance mode gating checkout-only, Telegram ops-notification setting); SET-4 (backup) and SET-8/9's actual sending mechanism excluded as genuinely blocked (deployment-pending, infra-doesn't-exist-yet respectively — same reasoning `ADR-015`/`ADR-016`/`ADR-020` already established against designing a setting for a mechanism that doesn't exist); SET-7/SET-11 stay in the existing Payment Methods tool, SET-3 stays Phase 2; `Reseller::platformOwner()` reused (not a second lookup mechanism) for the new public branding endpoint, its own doc comment to gain every call site once built so Phase 2's real domain-routing replacement doesn't miss one. **Design only, not built** — founder's own sequencing: grill the companion SEO Management ADR (§6.16 SEO-1..7) next, before building either.

**SEO Management — grilled and recorded as ADR-029, 2026-08-21 (same session, immediately after ADR-028).** Full 7-decision design in [ADR-029](./adr.md), not duplicated here — summary: per-game SEO (SEO-4) stays on `Game.seo_*`'s already-existing columns, wired into the existing `EditGameModal` rather than folded into any new table — resolves ADR-028's own open question by recognizing per-game and per-storefront SEO vary along different axes (which product vs. which reseller); SEO-2/3/6/7 land in a new `reseller_seo_settings` table (1:1 `Reseller`, kept separate from `reseller_branding` despite both being per-reseller — different concern, human-facing branding vs. machine-facing SEO/tracking) — default meta/OG, template patterns, typed GA/FB/TikTok pixel-ID columns plus a generic custom-script fallback; SEO-5 (redirects) gets its own reseller-scoped `redirects` table; SEO-1 (overview dashboard) needs no new storage at all, a computed coverage checklist, never a single invented "score". A real open question the founder asked directly — is a library needed, or build from scratch — got a researched, not assumed, answer: read this project's own bundled Next.js 16 docs (`admin/node_modules/next/dist/docs/`, per `admin/AGENTS.md`'s own warning that this version can differ from training data) rather than guessing; conclusion is exactly one new dependency worth adding (`@next/third-parties/google`, official Vercel package, not yet installed, for GA/GTM), everything else already native to this Next.js version (`generateMetadata()` for meta tags, `next/script` — already built-in — for FB/TikTok pixels and custom scripts). Access model (who actually edits these settings) also settled, applying identically to both ADR-028 and ADR-029: the Admin Panel edits on behalf of a reseller — today always `Reseller::platformOwner()` — since no reseller self-service portal exists yet (RES-1..6, Phase 2); both new tables carry a `reseller_id` FK so a future real portal reuses the same tables/endpoints scoped to the reseller's own id, no schema change needed then. **Design only, not built** — founder's own sequencing: build both ADR-028 and ADR-029 in a future session, one item at a time.

~~SEO-4 wired into the existing `EditGameModal`~~ — **superseded by a 2026-08-22 addendum, same day as the ADR-028 addendum below: per-game SEO gets its own "Game SEO" list+edit screen inside the new SEO module instead, `EditGameModal` untouched.** That addendum (grilled after reviewing a competing product's SEO admin panel, screenshots supplied by the founder — not this project's own data, critiqued item by item, not copied) also adds: structured data (JSON-LD Product/Organization/Breadcrumb schema, no new dependency), a `seo_scripts` table replacing the flat head/body-script field pair, native `app/robots.ts`-driven crawler/AI-bot management, a `hit_count` on redirects, and native `app/sitemap.ts` sitemap generation with a read-only status line + direct link on the Overview page — while explicitly rejecting the reference's vanity "score + letter grade", admin-configurable sitemap priority/changefreq, redirect regex matching, CSV import, and a platform-level analytics permission gate. Full decision-by-decision verdict in [ADR-029's second addendum](./adr.md). Still fully design-only, not built.

**ADR-025 (supplier price-sync sanity guard) built, 2026-08-21 (same day, later) — closes item 3 of the standing pointer above, no scope changes on contact with code, per two follow-up questions grilled and resolved as an ADR-025 addendum earlier this same session (see [ADR-025](./adr.md)).** Built test-first (`/mattpocock-skills:tdd`), exactly to the 8 original decisions plus that addendum's two resolutions (swing baseline confirmed safe by construction — `cost_price` has no admin-edit path — and the threshold stays flat-percentage, no absolute-sen floor added).

Backend: new `pending_price_changes` table (`price_sync_run_id` nullable-on-delete, `package_id` cascade, old/proposed cost+reseller price pairs, `status` — append-only, mirrors `price_change_logs`/`deactivation_logs`'s own convention), `App\Models\PendingPriceChange`. `PackagePriceSyncService::evaluatePriceChange()` now sits inside Price Propagation itself (not beside it): a floor violation (`price_sen <= 0`) logs and rejects unconditionally; a swing past `PRICE_SWING_THRESHOLD_PERCENT` (config `packages.price_swing_threshold_percent`, default 50) creates a `PendingPriceChange` row and deactivates the package under a new `deactivated_reason='price_anomaly'` (deliberately distinct from `'supplier_sync'` so `PendingReactivationFinder`'s existing filter never picks it up) — unless one is already unresolved for that package, per decision #5's idempotency guard. A package with no prior `cost_price` (`0`, legacy data) applies directly rather than divide-by-zero, same reasoning `promote()`'s own first-time creation already uses. One judgment call beyond the 8 recorded decisions, made during implementation: `flagPriceAnomaly()` only overwrites `is_active`/`deactivated_reason` when the package is currently active — mirrors `deactivate()`'s own existing "never touches a package the admin already deactivated" invariant, so a swing anomaly on an already admin-deactivated package still gets recorded (and is still reviewable) without silently clobbering the admin's own `deactivated_reason='admin'`. `SupplierProductController::promote()` gained the identical floor check (was previously only rejecting `price_sen === null`, not `=== 0`).

New `Middleware\PendingPriceChangeController` (index/approve/dismiss) under the existing `/middleware/price-sync` route group (already `super_admin`-only). Approve recomputes `reseller_cost_price` from the package's **live** `markup_percent` at approval time (decision #6 — proven by a test where the markup was edited after flagging) and writes a `PriceChangeLog` row so the audit trail stays complete; Dismiss reactivates at the untouched old price with no log write (nothing was ever actually applied). `PriceSyncController::stats()` gained `pending_price_change_count` (same live-count pattern as `pending_reactivation_count`); `SyncSupplierPricesJob`'s run `stats` gained `floor_rejected`/`price_anomalies` (decision #1's "surfaced on an existing stat card," realized as the Last Sync Details line rather than a new top-level card, to avoid cramping the existing 5-card row further than the one new Pending Price Change card already does).

Admin: new `PendingPriceChangeSection.tsx` (sixth Price Sync Center section, extracted as its own component from the start rather than inlined — `page.tsx` was already over this project's ~500-line guideline before this session), wired into `/middleware/price-sync` with a new "Pending Price Change" stat card and the Last Sync Details line now showing floor/anomaly counts when nonzero. The page's own intro copy corrected — it previously said "no approval gate on price either direction," no longer true now that a swing can gate one direction of the write.

24 new/changed backend tests across `PackagePriceSyncServiceTest` (+9), `SupplierProductControllerTest` (+1), new `PendingPriceChangeControllerTest` (7, including the standard `test_regular_admin_is_forbidden` case per this project's 2026-08-14 role-gate-audit convention), `PriceSyncControllerTest` (+1), `SyncSupplierPricesJobTest` (+1); full fast suite **614/614 green** (up from 598), full concurrency suite (real MySQL) still green. Migration verified via `SHOW INDEX FROM pending_price_changes` on real MySQL (both FK columns get their own standalone index, well under the 64-char identifier limit) and applied to the local dev DB (the documented gotcha, handled not hit blind). `npx tsc --noEmit`/`eslint`/`next build` clean on `admin/`.

**ADR-025 browser-verified live, 2026-08-21 (same day, later) — closes the AGENTS.md "start the dev server and use the feature in a browser" step, not done as part of the build entry above.** Founder asked directly whether this had actually been checked in a browser yet — it hadn't (only `tsc`/`eslint`/`next build`/the backend suite). Doing so surfaced an unrelated environmental problem first: ports 3000/8000 were serving a completely different, unrelated project ("nakhoda") whose dev servers happened to already be running — not this project's `admin/`/backend at all. Caught before any real login attempt succeeded against the wrong app; those processes were stopped (founder's own instruction) and this project's `admin/` dev server started fresh (`npm run dev`, landed on :3000 once the port was free) — the real backend was never affected, since it's served by Herd at `kedairuncit-backend.test`, not a manually-run `serve` process. With the right servers confirmed running: logged in as `test@example.com`, navigated to `/middleware/price-sync`, and — after seeding two throwaway flagged packages directly via `tinker` (cleaned up afterward, not left in the local dev DB) — clicked through both real actions against the real API: **Approve** correctly applied the proposed price and recomputed `reseller_cost_price` from live `markup_percent` (verified in the DB after, not just "the button didn't error"); **Dismiss** correctly reactivated at the old, untouched price. Both matched their respective backend tests' expectations exactly.

**Real bug found and fixed during that same browser verification, 2026-08-21 (same day, later) — `PendingReactivationFinder` was matching on stale evidence.** Clicking "Sync All Prices Now" live (to also exercise ADR-025's floor/swing path against a real sync, not just seeded data) surfaced a second, unrelated finding: it deactivated several genuinely-still-active packages, which then *immediately* reappeared in Pending Reactivation on the very same page load. Root cause, confirmed via a deliberate real (non-sandbox) sync against production Gamevion, not the broken 1-item sandbox catalog (`GAMEVION_SANDBOX` stays `true` in `.env` — this test only overrode `config()` at runtime in `tinker`, no `.env` write): `PackagePriceSyncService::apply()`'s "vanished from the catalog" deactivation path (`! $seenThisRun`) never touches that package's own `supplier_products` row — its `status_raw` is left exactly as it was *before* the deactivation, almost always `'active'` (the item was sellable up to that point). `PendingReactivationFinder::find()` matched on that raw `status_raw` alone with no freshness check, so the very evidence that predates a deactivation was being read as proof the item is active *again*.

Founder specifically asked whether this was really a bug or just an artifact of the local dev DB's last real sync being from 2026-07-24 (a month-old gap) — confirmed structural, not a staleness-duration artifact, via an isolated test using a deliberately short (20-minute) gap: `tests/Feature/Services/Sync/PendingReactivationFinderTest.php`'s first case reproduces the bug identically at 20 minutes as at a month, since the underlying mechanism (a `supplier_products` row simply never being touched by the run that deactivated its package) is time-independent. A second control case in the same file (genuine reactivation — a *later* sync run confirms `status_raw='active'` *after* the package's own `deactivated_at`) was written and confirmed passing against the **pre-fix** code first, proving the base mechanism itself is sound and the fix targets only the one real gap, not a rewrite.

Fix: `PendingReactivationFinder::find()` now additionally requires `SupplierProduct.last_synced_at` to be *after* the package's own `deactivated_at` — the 'active' signal must come from a sync that ran after the package went offline, not leftover state from before. `PriceSyncControllerTest::test_stats_reports_platform_wide_counts_and_last_run()`'s fixture updated to match (its `Pending`-package `deactivated_at` was previously the same instant as its `SupplierProduct.last_synced_at`, which happened to pass under the old, buggy code but is exactly the ambiguous case the fix now requires disambiguating with a real time gap). Full backend suite **616/616 green** (up from 614). Verified against real local dev DB data too: the 5 packages the real Gamevion sync above had actually mis-flagged dropped from 5 to 0 in `PendingReactivationFinder`'s live count immediately after the fix, no code path bypassed, and re-confirmed a second time live in the browser after the fix (Pending Reactivation showing 0, the real Run #8 history untouched). No ADR opened — this closes a gap in an already-decided design (ADR-015 decision #3), not a new architectural trade-off.

~~**➡️ NEXT SESSION STARTS HERE (2026-08-21 pointer, updated after ADR-025 shipped + browser-verified and the PendingReactivationFinder bug fixed).** The 2026-08-14 pointer's remaining items are all still open and unaffected by this session's work — see items 1/2/5/6/7/8 above (item 3, ADR-025, is now the only one struck as done) — plus one new, small item this session surfaced:~~ Superseded by the later 2026-08-21 pointer further below — items 4/5/8/9 (and item 3 already) are now closed too; item 9 is listed just below, kept for its own record.

9. ~~**`PendingReactivationFinder`'s fix is a good candidate for the same short-gap-repro treatment applied to `PendingPriceChange`'s own idempotency guard** (flagged as an untested concurrency gap in ADR-025's own Consequence to track) — not urgent, both are logic-level correctness gaps rather than money-moving races, but worth a look together next time either area is touched.~~ **Closed, 2026-08-21 (new session) — see the entry below: `PendingPriceChange` half fixed, `PendingReactivationFinder` half confirmed not applicable, not silently dropped.**

**`PendingPriceChange`'s idempotency guard (ADR-025 decision #5) closed a real, confirmed race, 2026-08-21 (new session) — closes item 9's `PendingPriceChange` half of the standing pointer above.** ADR-025's own Consequence to track flagged this as untested-under-genuine-concurrency; verified it wasn't just theoretical before fixing. `PackagePriceSyncService::evaluatePriceChange()`'s swing branch did a plain "does a pending row already exist?" `exists()` check, then later `PendingPriceChange::create()`, with no DB-level lock between the two — two overlapping sync runs (a scheduled run + a manual "Sync All Prices Now" click, or two admins clicking at once) evaluating the same package could both pass the check before either inserted, producing two open `pending_price_changes` rows for one package (breaks decision #5's "one open anomaly per package" invariant). Confirmed real, not hypothetical: temporarily widened the race window (`usleep(200000)` between the check and the write, lock removed) and the new concurrency test below reliably reproduced 2 flagged rows for 1 package — reverted immediately after confirming, not left in.

Fix: wrapped the check + `flagPriceAnomaly()` write in `DB::transaction()` with `Package::lockForUpdate()` on the package row itself — no new lock primitive, matches this codebase's existing row-lock convention (`LedgerService::withdraw()`/`VoucherService::redeem()`, `backend/CLAUDE.md`). New `tests/Concurrency/PendingPriceChangeConcurrencyTest.php` (real MySQL via `docker-compose.yml`, spawns two genuine subprocesses via a new test-only `app:price-sync-test-evaluate` command, same pattern as `VoucherRedeemConcurrencyTest`/`app:voucher-test-redeem`) — asserts exactly one of the two processes flags the anomaly and exactly one `pending_price_changes` row exists after both complete. Full concurrency suite **5/5 green** (up from 4); full fast suite unaffected, still 618/618.

**`PendingReactivationFinder`'s half of item 9 investigated and closed as not-applicable, same session — checked, not assumed.** `SyncSupplierPricesJob` (the only caller of `PackagePriceSyncService::apply()` anywhere in the codebase) runs on its own dedicated `price-sync` queue, and `docker-compose.prod.yml`'s `queue-worker-price-sync` service is deliberately kept single-replica (unlike `queue-worker-orders`, whose own comment explicitly names future 2-replica scaling as a real, expected option) — so in this project's actual deployed and locally-run topology, `apply()` can never execute concurrently with itself; jobs on that queue process strictly one at a time. That reframes the `PendingPriceChange` fix above too: the race was real and reproducible at the code level (proven by forcing it with two raw subprocesses bypassing the queue entirely), a legitimate defensive fix worth having, but not an active production risk today given the single-worker topology — recorded here so a future reader doesn't mistake it for a live incident. `PendingReactivationFinder` itself is read-only (a SELECT, no write path of its own); the two columns its query depends on (`Package.deactivated_at`, `SupplierProduct.last_synced_at`) are both written inside that same single, serialized job run (Stage 1 then Stage 2, one worker) — there is no second concurrent writer for the check-then-write race pattern to apply to, unlike `PendingPriceChange`'s own table. The one genuinely concurrent writer to the same `Package` row — an admin manually toggling `is_active` via `/admin/games` (a synchronous HTTP request) while the queue worker processes a sync job on the same package — is a materially different class of race (admin-vs-background-job, not two queue jobs racing each other) that already has partial protection in the existing code (`PackagePriceSyncService::deactivate()` never overwrites a package an admin already deactivated, `deactivated_reason === 'admin'`) and was never what item 9's own wording was pointing at. No code change needed; closing this as a confirmed non-gap rather than an open item.

**6 (actually 8) inline `validate()` endpoints converted to FormRequest, 2026-08-21 (new session) — closes item 4's FormRequest sub-item from the standing pointer above, no functional change.** A repo-wide grep for `$request->validate(` found 8 mutating routes still validating inline against this codebase's own "every mutating route gets a FormRequest" convention (`backend/CLAUDE.md`), one more than the pointer's own count: `PackageController::updateStatus`, `Admin\HeroSlideController::updateStatus`, `Admin\AdminUserController::updateStatus`, `Admin\WithdrawalController::reject`, `Admin\VoucherController::storeFromOrder`, `Middleware\PaymentMethodController::updateStatus`, `Middleware\PaymentMethodController::updateFee` (the second of the pointer's undercount), `Middleware\PlayerValidatorProfileController::test`. All were small, single-purpose toggles/fields (`is_active` booleans, a nullable note/reason string, fee numerics, a player/server ID pair) — extracted into 8 new FormRequest classes (`UpdatePackageStatusRequest`, `UpdateHeroSlideStatusRequest`, `UpdateAdminUserStatusRequest`, `RejectWithdrawalRequest`, `StoreVoucherFromOrderRequest`, `UpdatePaymentMethodStatusRequest`, `UpdatePaymentMethodFeeRequest`, `TestPlayerValidatorProfileRequest`), same validation rules verbatim, no behavior change. Unused `Illuminate\Http\Request` imports dropped where the controller had no other route still needing it (`PackageController`, `PlayerValidatorProfileController`); left in place elsewhere (`index()`/`test()` on `PaymentMethodController`, `revoke()` on `VoucherController`, `complete()`/`approve()` on `WithdrawalController` still take a plain `Request`). No new tests needed — existing route tests for these 8 endpoints already exercise the same validation rules through the HTTP layer, unaffected by where the rules live; full suite still **618/618 green**, unchanged.

**`channel_properties` inner-key allowlist closed, 2026-08-21 (new session) — closes item 4's `channel_properties` sub-item from the standing pointer above.** No ADR needed — a straightforward input-validation-at-the-boundary gap (AGENTS.md's own rule), not an architectural trade-off. `CreateCheckoutRequest`'s `channel_properties` rule previously only checked `'nullable','array'`, with no check on what inner keys it held; the storefront (`OrderForm.tsx`) has only ever sent `success_return_url`/`failure_return_url`, and both get overwritten server-side anyway (`CheckoutService::requestPayment()`, per the 2026-07-29 post-payment-redirect fix above) — so a direct `POST /api/checkout` caller bypassing the storefront had no boundary check stopping it from stuffing arbitrary extra keys into the array, which then flowed straight into `XenditGateway::createPaymentRequest()`'s real Xendit payload unfiltered. Not a money-bypass (price/profit stay server-computed regardless, ORD-9), but a real gap against this project's own "validate input at system boundaries" convention. Fixed with a closure-based validation rule rejecting (`422`) any key outside the two-item allowlist — no new file, no schema change. 2 new `CheckoutControllerTest` cases (`test_rejects_channel_properties_with_an_unsupported_key`, `test_accepts_channel_properties_with_only_allowlisted_keys`); full backend suite **618/618 green** (up from 616).

**`XENDIT_TEST_SECRET_KEY` repo secret closed, 2026-08-21 (new session) — closes item 5.** Founder confirmed `backend/.env`'s existing `XENDIT_SECRET_KEY` already is a real test-mode key (per the 2026-07-29 live e2e session's own finding, cited in ADR-023's addendum above) — nothing new to obtain. Set via `gh secret set XENDIT_TEST_SECRET_KEY --repo farresyh/kedairuncitsoloz`, value piped directly from `.env` to `gh` without ever passing through the assistant's own context (sandbox policy also blocks direct `.env` reads, independently enforcing the same discipline). Unblocks the `playwright` CI job's storefront checkout spec.

**4th E2E golden path (`needs_review` → Mark as Delivered) built, 2026-08-21 (new session) — closes item 8.** Full detail — including the 2 real UI bugs found and fixed (`OrderController::markDelivered()` missing eager-loaded relations; the success message unmounting itself the instant `delivery_status` left the failed/needs_review bucket) and the pre-existing `next dev` auth-race this session's 3rd admin spec exposed (root-caused and fixed via a real `networkidle` synchronization point in `loginAsAdmin()`, not a sleep) — is in [ADR-023's newest addendum](./adr.md), not duplicated here. New `e2e/tests/admin-mark-delivered.spec.ts`; `E2ESeeder` gained a third, fully-independent fixture order. Backend: `resend_attempts`/`game`/`package`/`supplier`/`reseller`/`voucher` now eager-loaded on `markDelivered()`'s response, 2 new `OrderControllerTest` assertions, full suite **618/618 green** (assertion count up from 1645 to 1651). **ADR-028 (Platform & Reseller Settings) grilled further and built, 2026-08-22 (new session) — closes item 9 of the standing pointer above, and reframes items 1/2 as genuinely done, not just reframed.** Reviewing a mockup of the Settings screen with the founder surfaced real gaps the original ADR-028 decision 2 hadn't covered — an About Us page, footer content deserving its own table/tab, rich-text formatting, and a `{store_name}` template variable — grilled and recorded as a same-ADR addendum (decisions 10–16, not a new ADR number) in [ADR-028](./adr.md), then built test-first the same session.

Backend: three new tables — `reseller_branding` (store identity/contact, 1:1 `Reseller`), `reseller_footer_settings` (footer text + T&C/Privacy/About Us content + an ordered `footer_game_ids` JSON array, 1:1 `Reseller`), `platform_settings` (singleton — currency, maintenance mode, Telegram ops-notification config). `mews/purifier` added as this codebase's first HTML sanitizer (a named `rich_text` config profile), sanitizing legal content on save and again defensively at the point it's served (`BrandingController::legal()`) — belt and braces, not trusting stored data forever just because it was clean once. `{store_name}` is a literal token, substituted only at serve time (never at save time, so a later store-name change is reflected automatically), via a small `str_replace()` — no templating engine needed for one token. `SettingsController::bulkMarkup()` (SET-2's "apply to all packages" action, mechanics decided at build time per the addendum's own deferral) mirrors `PackageController::updateMarkup()`'s existing `PriceChangeLog` write exactly, one row per package actually changed, wrapped in `DB::transaction()` with `chunkById()`. `CheckoutController::store()` now enforces maintenance mode (ADR-028 decision 8, was already designed, not previously wired) — blocks new checkout submissions only, browsing/tracking/webhooks stay live, per that decision's own reasoning. `Reseller::platformOwner()`'s doc comment updated per decision 9 to list every call site, now including `BrandingController` and `Admin\SettingsController`. All three admin write routes gated `super_admin`-only, same tier as the Price Sync Center, with the standard `test_regular_admin_is_forbidden` case per this project's 2026-08-14 role-gate-audit convention.

Admin: new `/admin/settings`, three tabs (`StoreBrandingSection`/`FooterSettingsSection`/`PlatformSettingsSection`, each its own component per this project's ~500-line file guideline, same reasoning `PendingPriceChangeSection` was already extracted for). New shared `admin/src/components/ui/RichTextEditor.tsx` (`@tiptap/react` + `@tiptap/starter-kit` + `@tiptap/extension-underline` + `@tiptap/extension-link`, addendum decision 16 — a reusable primitive from day one, not inlined, justified by this feature's own 3× internal reuse across T&C/Privacy/About Us) — each legal field also renders a live `{store_name}`-resolved preview beneath the editor for proofreading, matching decision 13 exactly. Footer Games section: a checkbox grid of active games, hard-capped at 4 (remaining checkboxes disable once 4 are picked, a live "N / 4 selected" counter), preserving pick order for the storefront's display order.

Storefront: new `GET /api/catalog/branding` (branding + resolved footer games) and `GET /api/catalog/legal/{page}` (sanitized + substituted legal content, public/guest, same no-auth reasoning as `CatalogController`/`HeroSlideController` per ADR-011). Three new routes — `storefront/src/app/terms/page.tsx`, `.../privacy/page.tsx`, `.../about-us/page.tsx` — the first use of `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` anywhere in this codebase (a documented, deliberate exception: content is sanitized server-side twice before it ever reaches the render, not merely because it's admin-authored). `SiteFooter.tsx`'s hardcoded `FOOTER_COLUMNS`/`SOCIAL_LINKS`/copyright text and hardcoded "Products" game list are gone — real fetched data throughout, "About Us"/"Terms & Conditions"/"Privacy Policy" are now real links, `footerGames.length === 0` hides the "Top Up Games" block entirely rather than showing an empty heading (addendum decision 14). `ReviewModal.tsx`'s T&C checkbox text now links to `/terms` (opens in a new tab, stops click propagation so the link doesn't also toggle the checkbox) — it was plain unlinked text before this session, closing a real gap the original mockup review surfaced. Every page rendering `SiteFooter` needing live data gained `export const dynamic = "force-dynamic"` (never bake admin-editable content into a static build artifact).

**A real bug found and fixed during browser verification, same session — `Cache::remember()` was caching a raw `Collection` of `Game` models, not a plain array.** `backend/CLAUDE.md` already documents this exact convention ("`Cache::remember()` values must be plain arrays, never a raw Eloquent Model/Collection... the `database` cache driver silently corrupts nested objects on the next read" — ADR-014's own addendum) — `BrandingController::show()`'s first draft violated it anyway. Symptom, confirmed live: the *first* request after a cache miss correctly returned all 4 picked footer games; every request after that, for the rest of the 60-second TTL, silently returned a truncated list (`Cache::get()` unserializing the stored `Game` models into `__PHP_Incomplete_Class` stubs). Caught by hand — a single-request feature test can't catch a cache-HIT-only bug — while manually saving real Footer Settings content and reloading `/terms` twice in a real browser (the AGENTS.md-mandated step the build session itself hadn't finished before reporting done). Fixed with one `->toArray()` call before the payload leaves the `Cache::remember` closure; new regression test `BrandingControllerTest::test_show_returns_the_same_footer_games_on_a_cached_second_request` calls the endpoint twice in one test specifically to exercise the cache-HIT path, since the existing single-call tests structurally couldn't have caught this.

18 new/changed backend tests across `SettingsControllerTest` (10, incl. the standard forbidden-role case), `BrandingControllerTest` (7, incl. the new cache regression test), plus the `CheckoutControllerTest` maintenance-mode case; full backend suite **636/636 green** (up from 618). `npx tsc --noEmit`/`next build` clean on both `admin/` and `storefront/`. Migrations applied to the real local dev DB (the documented gotcha, handled not hit blind). Browser-verified for real, end to end, by hand: logged into `/admin/settings`, filled and saved all three tabs (including a `{store_name}` token and a bolded sentence in Terms & Conditions, 4 Footer Games at the cap), then loaded `/terms`, `/privacy`, `/about-us` and the storefront footer and confirmed the token resolved, formatting survived the sanitize round-trip, and all 4 games rendered in the picked order — catching the cache bug above in the process, not just confirming the happy path once.

**ADR-029 (SEO Management) built, 2026-08-22 (new session — ADR-028's own build above had already shipped and been committed before this session started).** All 20 decisions across the ADR and its two addendums built in one pass, backend through storefront, per the founder's own "full build, sequenced, confirm/ask questions first" instruction.

Backend: four new tables — `reseller_seo_settings` (1:1 `Reseller` — default meta/OG, `{game_name}`/`{store_name}` template patterns reusing ADR-028 addendum decision 13's exact convention, GA/FB/TikTok pixel IDs, JSON-LD toggles), `redirects` (reseller-scoped, exact-path only per addendum 2 decision 15, `hit_count`), `seo_scripts` (ordered head/body_end scripts, nullable `reseller_id` = global, tag-balance-only validation per its own documented scope limit), `crawler_rules` (deliberately **not** reseller-scoped — robots.txt is one file per domain, a build-time call the migration's own doc comment records). `Game` gained `schema_brand`/`schema_category`/`no_index`. New `SeoController` (public, guest-callable, cached+`forgetCache()` mirroring `BrandingController`) plus five `Admin\*` controllers (`SeoController`, `GameSeoController`, `RedirectController`, `SeoScriptController`, `CrawlerRuleController`), all `admin.role:super_admin`-gated same tier as Settings. `CatalogController::publicGameDetail()` extended with the three new columns. 636/636 backend tests still green (no new backend tests added this pass — plain CRUD/cache mirroring existing, already-covered patterns; worth a follow-up test pass before this ships to a second reseller).

Admin: new collapsible "SEO" nav group (`AppSidebar.tsx`'s first non-flat nav item) — Overview (computed checklist + severity-tagged Recommendations, explicitly no weighted score per decision 4/addendum 2 decision 17), Global Settings, Meta Templates, Game SEO (its own list+edit screen per addendum 2 decision 11, char counters at Google's real 70/160 truncation points, "Use Game Image" button, `no_index` toggle), Redirects, Scripts, Crawler — 7 pages, all list/CRUD pages mirroring `/admin/blacklist`'s existing table+modal pattern. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean.

Storefront: `/order/[slug]`'s `generateMetadata()` now chains per-game override → template (substituted via a small shared `renderTemplate()`) → storefront default → the original hardcoded fallback, plus a `no_index` → `robots: { index: false }` mapping and Product/Breadcrumb JSON-LD (toggled per `reseller_seo_settings`). Root `layout.tsx` gained dynamic `generateMetadata()`, Organization JSON-LD, `@next/third-parties/google`'s `<GoogleAnalytics>` (confirmed still experimental-but-officially-maintained via a fresh npm/docs check before installing, per the ADR's own instruction to re-verify at build time), and `next/script`-based FB/TikTok pixels + admin-authored scripts. New native `app/robots.ts`/`app/sitemap.ts` (both `force-dynamic` — a redirect/game-list change shouldn't need a rebuild to take effect; sitemap has no admin-configurable priority/changefreq, addendum 2 decision 16). **Redirects execute via a new `src/proxy.ts`, not `middleware.ts`** — this project's installed Next.js 16 has deprecated and renamed the file convention (function name `proxy`, not `middleware`), confirmed by reading this project's own bundled docs rather than assuming, exactly the discipline `storefront/AGENTS.md` mandates; an in-memory 60s-TTL cache keyed by `from_path`, populated from the new public `/api/catalog/seo/redirects` endpoint, with a fire-and-forget hit-count POST on match.

Two build-time environment fixes surfaced and fixed, not routed around: (1) `npm install` run from the repo root instead of `storefront/` accidentally created a stray root `package.json`/`package-lock.json`/`node_modules` holding just `@next/third-parties` — caught via `git status` before committing anything, deleted, reinstalled correctly inside `storefront/`. (2) `storefront/package.json`'s `build` script never carried the `NODE_OPTIONS=--use-system-ca` flag `dev` already has (for Herd's local HTTPS cert) — never mattered before because no page fetched from the backend at build time; the new root `layout.tsx` now does (branding/SEO settings/scripts, on every page), so `next build` started failing on the untrusted local cert. Fixed by adding the same flag to `build`, matching `dev`'s already-established solution to the identical problem, not inventing a new one.

Deliberately not built: addendum 2 decision 17's "Recent Issues" table — no real SEO issue/event log exists yet to back a scannable per-row history (unlike blacklist's block-attempt log or price-sync's run history); Overview ships Recommendations only. Worth building once a real signal exists to populate it (e.g. failed sitemap fetches, redirect-loop detection), not fabricated now for its own sake.

**Browser-verified for real, same session, immediately after — the founder asked directly how to confirm technical settings actually work, not just take a green build on faith.** New `CrawlerRuleSeeder` (called from `DatabaseSeeder`) seeds the "curated default bot list" addendum 2 decision 14 calls for — search engines + social-preview bots (`facebookexternalhit`/`Twitterbot`/`LinkedInBot`/`WhatsApp`) allowed, named AI-training bots (`GPTBot`/`ChatGPT-User`/`CCBot`/`ClaudeBot`/`Google-Extended`/`Bytespider`/`PerplexityBot`) disallowed by default, a `*` catch-all — the `crawler_rules` table was otherwise empty on a fresh install, `/robots.txt` would've shipped with nothing to show or edit. Logged into `/admin/seo` for real (`test@example.com`/`password`, the standing seeder account) and clicked through: Overview's stat cards and Recommendations matched the real DB exactly (4 active games, 9 sitemap URLs = 5 static + 4 games, verified against a direct `curl` of `/sitemap.xml`); added a real custom crawler rule, confirmed it round-tripped to a live `curl` of `/robots.txt`, deleted it again; edited Free Fire (Malaysia)'s SEO title end-to-end and confirmed the live `/order/free-fire-malaysia` page's `<title>` tag changed to match, then cleared it back to empty.

Two real bugs this surfaced and fixed, not just typechecked around:
1. **A crawler-rule edit didn't show up on `/robots.txt` for up to 60 seconds.** `storefront/src/lib/seo.ts`'s three fetch helpers (`getSeoSettings`/`getSeoScripts`/`getCrawlerRules`) all carried `next: { revalidate: 60 }` — Next.js's own fetch-level cache, stacked *on top of* Laravel's already-invalidated `Cache::remember()`. Every call site (root layout, `/order/[slug]`, `app/robots.ts`, `app/sitemap.ts`) is already a per-request dynamic route, so this second cache layer only added stale-for-up-to-a-minute behavior with zero benefit. Removed; an admin save now reflects immediately, confirmed live.
2. **Overview's "Fix Now" links pointed at the wrong filter.** `filter=incomplete` means "one of title/description is set, the other missing" — a fresh catalog (every game's both fields null) is `filter=missing` instead, and the "missing OG image" recommendation doesn't map onto either status at all (OG image isn't part of that 3-way classification). Clicking "Fix Now" from a real "4 games missing an SEO title" recommendation showed "No games match." Fixed by linking all three recommendations to the unfiltered `/admin/seo/games` list instead of guessing a status that doesn't reliably match — status badges per row make the actual gap visible without a magic (and sometimes wrong) deep-link.

One process mistake worth recording so it isn't repeated: while restarting a dev server to test, killed the wrong PID (the founder's own already-running `/admin` dev server on port 3000, misread from an ambiguous log line) instead of a redundant one this session had just spawned — caught immediately because the next navigation failed, restarted it right away, no data lost, but a reminder to read `npm run dev`'s port-conflict output carefully before killing anything.

**Crawler: one shared default-disallow field added, ADR-029 addendum 3 (2026-08-22, same session, founder-requested mid-verification).** Walking through `/admin/seo/crawler` live, the founder asked the right question: block one path for every bot, without editing every row. The honest answer under the original per-row `disallow_paths` design was "yes, one by one" — a robots.txt `User-agent` block never inherits from `*`. Added `reseller_seo_settings.crawler_default_disallow_paths` instead, merged into every allowed bot's own disallow list server-side (`SeoController::robots()`), covering bots added later automatically. New "Default Disallow Paths (all bots)" section on the Crawler page; seeded with `/order/status` (a specific customer's order — no SEO value, real privacy concern if indexed) and `/api` (this app's own Next.js route handlers). Browser-verified live: edited the field, confirmed via direct DOM `.click()` (the automation tool's coordinate-based click had a timing quirk against this specific button — a session/tooling issue, not an app bug, confirmed by the same click working via `element.click()`) that the save round-tripped to the DB, then to a live `curl` of `/robots.txt` showing the new path appended to `*`, `Googlebot`, and `Bingbot` alike, then cleared the test value back to the real default.

**`llms.txt` added, ADR-029 addendum 4 (2026-08-22, same session, founder-asked).** The founder asked directly whether `llms.txt` (llmstxt.org's proposed markdown AI-context convention) was the same thing as `robots.txt` — it isn't: `robots.txt` is access control, `llms.txt` is a curated content digest for LLM consumption, a genuinely different concern that happens to also be crawler-facing. Scoped down to two quick decisions in chat rather than a full grilling pass (small surface, not a multi-table feature): fully auto-generated from existing catalog/branding data (same no-new-table choice `app/sitemap.ts` already made — nothing to drift out of sync), and only the concise `/llms.txt` convention, not the less-established `/llms-full.txt` variant. New `storefront/src/app/llms.txt/route.ts` (a plain Route Handler, `force-dynamic`, `text/markdown` response) — store name as H1, a summary line (`default_meta_description` → `branding.description` → a generic fallback), every active game as a link with category + price-from, then the four static pages (`/track-order`, `/about-us`, `/terms`, `/privacy`). `tsc`/`lint`/`next build` clean; browser/`curl`-verified live at `/llms.txt` against the real 4-game catalog. Genuinely unproven convention — no confirmed universal consumer today the way `robots.txt` has — built because it's cheap and harmless, not because adoption is established; see [ADR-029's fourth addendum](./adr.md) for the full reasoning.

**Dedup mechanics confirmed with the founder, same session (Q&A, no code change):** the `(game_id, denomination)` grouping is genuinely N-way, not pairwise — however many active packages share a denomination (any mix of suppliers), exactly the cheapest one shows. The pick is **not sticky** — it's recomputed fresh from live `cost_price`/`markup_percent` on every cache-cold read, and `SyncSupplierPricesJob` (the price-propagation job) already calls `GameController::forgetPackagesCache()`/`forgetIndexCache()` after every run, so a price change from a routine Price Sync flips the storefront's pick automatically — no admin action, no code change needed for this to work correctly.

**Session summary, 2026-08-26 (same session, continued) — ADR-036 (Voucher Merge) built, no scope changes on contact with code.** `voucher_merges` table (`source_voucher_id`/`target_voucher_id` FK `vouchers` cascadeOnDelete, `reason`, `merged_by`, `timestamps()`). `VoucherService::merge()` mirrors `restore()`'s no-ledger-write shape: locks every selected voucher, verifies all `active` and same-customer (the existing email/phone equivalence rule, extended pairwise), sums `remaining`, creates the new voucher directly (bypassing `issue()`'s ledger call), voids each source (`remaining = 0`, `status = 'merged'`, never deleted). New `POST /api/vouchers/merge` (`VoucherController::merge()`, `MergeVouchersRequest`), same `admin.role:super_admin,admin` gate as every other voucher route — no maker-checker, per decision 7. `Voucher` model gained `mergesAsTarget()`/`mergeAsSource()` relations, eager-loaded on `show()`. Admin: `/admin/vouchers` gained per-row checkboxes (active vouchers only) + "Merge Selected" button, `MergeVouchersModal.tsx` (mirrors `CreateVoucherModal.tsx`), and the detail view gained a "Merge History" section. 10 new backend tests; full backend suite **730/730 green** (up from 723); `admin/` `tsc --noEmit`/`next build` clean. Migration applied to the local dev DB before closing out.

**Live-verified for real in a browser, same session, at the founder's request — not just typechecked.** Started `admin/`'s dev server against the real local backend (already live via Herd at `kedairuncit-backend.test`, no `php artisan serve` needed — merge is synchronous, no queue job involved). Founder then asked what a customer sees trying to use a voided source at checkout: confirmed live via `POST /api/vouchers/preview` (the storefront's real Apply-button endpoint) — a voided source returns the identical generic `422` "This voucher code is not valid for this order." every other invalid-voucher reason already returns (ADR-024's own deliberate indistinguishability, `VoucherService::assertUsable()` — ADR-036 didn't change this, a merged voucher just falls into the existing `status !== 'active'` branch), while the merge's own new voucher previews correctly (`200`, real discount). No system-surfaced hint of the new code — the customer must be told out-of-band by the admin, matching this feature's own manual/opt-in design.

**Session summary, 2026-08-26 (new session) — `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint fix built across all 28 files (ADR-038 decision 8), closing item 4 of the pointer above.** New `admin/src/hooks/useClientSession.ts` — a `useSyncExternalStore`-based hook replacing the duplicated `useState<SessionPayload|null>(null)` + `useEffect(() => setSession(getClientSession()), [])` shape everywhere it was only used to feed *render* (JSX, handlers): `UserDropdown.tsx` and, for that purpose only, all 23 gated `page.tsx` files. `lib/session.ts`'s `setClientSession()`/`clearClientSession()` now dispatch a new `SESSION_CHANGE_EVENT` so the hook picks up a same-tab login/logout immediately (the native `storage` event only fires in *other* tabs).

**Real regression caught live, not just typechecked — the exact bug class the Backups page's earlier reverted attempt (previous entry above) ran into.** Making the mount effect's own auth-redirect check depend on `useClientSession()`'s value (`if (!session) router.replace("/login")`, keyed off the hook) raced the hook's post-hydration resync on a hard/direct navigation: browser-verified with a real Sanctum token injected into `sessionStorage` and a hard navigation straight to `/admin/blacklist` — the page redirected to `/login` despite a genuinely valid session sitting right there in storage. Root cause: a plain `getClientSession()` call inside a mount effect (the original, pre-ADR-038 shape) is already synchronous/authoritative the moment that effect runs — no hydration ambiguity exists there at all — while the hook's client snapshot is only guaranteed correct *for rendering*, not for a same-tick effect-only decision. Fix: every gated page's mount effect keeps its own direct `getClientSession()` read for the redirect + initial fetch (unchanged from the original code, just minus the now-redundant `setSession(s)` call); `useClientSession()` is used only where render/handlers need the value afterward. Re-verified live both directions (valid session persists across a hard reload; cleared session redirects correctly) with zero hydration warnings either way before rolling the pattern out to the other 22 pages.

**Second real gap found beyond the pre-identified fix, affecting 6 pages' `refresh()` helpers (hero-slides, seo/crawler, seo/games, seo/redirects, seo/scripts, settings) — the lint rule's static analysis does not see through `async`/`await`.** A `refresh(token)` helper written as `async function refresh(token) { try { setX(await y(token)); } catch { setError(...); } }`, called bare from an effect, is still flagged even though the identical logic written as a `.then()`/`.catch()` promise chain (`y(token).then(setX).catch(...)`, the shape blacklist's inline fetch and `validators/page.tsx`'s pre-existing `load()` helper both already used) is not — confirmed by toggling one file between the two shapes and re-running eslint. Fixed by converting all 6 `refresh()` helpers from try/await/catch to a returned `.then/.catch` chain; every existing `await refresh(token)` caller elsewhere in each file keeps working unchanged since the function still returns a `Promise`.

**Third gap, 2 files: `orders/page.tsx` and `sandbox/page.tsx` both reset pagination to page 1 via a synchronous `setPageNumber(1)` inside an effect keyed on the search/status filters** — the actual "adjust state when a prop/other state changes" case React's own docs cover, fixed with the documented pattern (compare against a tracked previous filter value during render, not in an effect) rather than a `key`-remount (pagination state needs to survive the rest of the page, unlike the modal-content cases below).

**Fourth (pre-identified) gap, 3 modal files — `SyncDetailsModal.tsx`, `BlacklistHitsModal.tsx`, `CreateSandboxOrderModal.tsx`.** The first two's "reset then fetch on open" effects were restructured into a small content component keyed by the entity id (`key={runId}`/`key={entryId}`), rendered only while open — the reset disappears entirely since a fresh mount already starts at the initial state, per ADR-038 decision 8's own "key-based remount" plan. `CreateSandboxOrderModal`'s game→package reset used the same render-time-adjustment pattern as the pagination fix above, since it's genuinely a same-component derived-state reset, not an open/close remount case.

**Storefront: `TrackOrderClient.tsx`'s prefill auto-lookup hit the same async/await-vs-promise-chain issue as the admin `refresh()` helpers**, even after splitting the synchronous submit-time `setLoading(true)`/`setError(null)` out via a lazy `useState` initializer seeded from the URL param — calling the (async, `await`-based) `doLookup()` helper from the effect was still flagged. Fixed the same way: the prefill effect inlines `trackOrder(trimmed).then(...).catch(...)` directly rather than calling `doLookup()`.

One pre-existing, unrelated lint error fixed in passing since it was in a file already being touched and blocking the same `npm run lint` from going green: `seo/settings/page.tsx`'s unescaped apostrophe (`react/no-unescaped-entities`).

**Verification: `npm run lint` — 0 errors, 0 warnings, both `admin/` and `storefront/`. `tsc --noEmit` clean both. `next build` clean both, route list unchanged (no build regressions).** Live-verified in a browser beyond the redirect-race check above: `/admin/orders`' status-filter buttons and `/middleware/sandbox`'s "Create Test Order" modal (the `CreateSandboxOrderModal` render-time-adjustment fix) both exercised with zero console errors and no runaway re-render loop. Backend/E2E suites untouched — this was a frontend-only fix, no backend code changed.

**Session summary, 2026-08-27 (new session) — playwright CI job's "Process from config.webServer was not able to start. Exit code: 1" (pointer item 4, outstanding since 2026-08-26) finally root-caused and fixed.** Never reproducible locally (a fresh `git worktree` with zero `.env` anywhere, mirroring CI exactly, booted and passed 3/4 specs on the first try) — root-caused instead by temporarily adding `DEBUG=pw:webserver` to the CI job itself (pushed, observed, then reverted in a second commit) since Playwright swallows webServer stdout/stderr on this failure path by default. Real cause: `e2e/scripts/boot-backend.sh`'s `export APP_KEY="${APP_KEY:-$(php artisan key:generate --show)}"` — Symfony Console detects the `GITHUB_ACTIONS` env var and force-decorates output with ANSI codes even though this command's stdout is being captured via `$()`, not a real TTY; `key:generate --show` wraps its output in a `<comment>` tag, so the captured `APP_KEY` on CI silently contained escape-sequence bytes around the base64 key. Harmless until the first real encrypted write — surfaced only when `E2ESeeder` writes `Supplier.api_config` (encrypted cast, ADR-046) as `RuntimeException: Unsupported cipher or incorrect key length`, which Playwright then flattened into its generic exit-1 message. Fix: `--no-ansi` appended to the `key:generate --show` call. Verified live on PR #11's CI: `playwright` job now boots all 3 webServers and passes 3/4 specs (Issue Voucher, Mark as Delivered, Resend Delivery) — the 4th (`storefront-checkout.spec.ts`) still fails, but at the real Xendit API call, exactly the pre-existing/documented `XENDIT_TEST_SECRET_KEY` repo-secret gap ci.yml's own comment already predicted, not a new bug. `backend-tests`/`backend-concurrency`/`admin`/`storefront` jobs unaffected, all green same run.

**Same session, same day — founder added `XENDIT_TEST_SECRET_KEY` as a real repo secret, re-running PR #11's CI surfaced a second, genuinely separate bug: `/login`'s own rate limit isn't actually isolated to `/login`.** `Illuminate\Routing\Middleware\ThrottleRequests::resolveRequestSignature()` keys every `throttle:N,1` bucket as `sha1($route->getDomain().'|'.$request->ip())` — the route path is never part of the key — so every throttled route in `routes/api.php` that didn't pass the middleware's own optional 3rd `prefix` argument (`/login`, `/checkout`, `/client-errors`, `/vouchers/preview`, `/games/{game}/validate-player`, `/track-order/{orderNumber}`, `/seo/redirects/record-hit`, both webhook routes) shared one counter per IP, regardless of each route's own configured `maxAttempts`. `storefront-checkout.spec.ts`'s own admin-API login got a genuine 429 after only 1 real login attempt in that run — the storefront page's `/client-errors` beacons (an unrelated pre-existing `BrandingWire`/`social_links` zod schema-drift warning, itself not investigated this session) and the checkout POST itself had already spent most of `/login`'s 5/minute budget from the same IP. This isn't E2E-only: a real guest browsing the storefront and an admin logging in from the same IP (an office NAT, a founder testing checkout and admin from the same laptop) hit the identical bug in production. Fix: every `throttle:` route now carries its own prefix (`throttle:5,1,login`, `throttle:30,1,client-errors`, etc.), isolating each route's bucket the way `maxAttempts` was always meant to be read. New regression test (`AuthControllerTest::test_login_rate_limit_is_not_shared_with_other_throttled_routes`) pins `/login` staying open after 10 hits on `/client-errors` from the same IP. 881/881 backend suite green. PR #11's CI now fully green, including all 4 `playwright` specs.

Root cause since found (same session as the ADR-047/048 grill below, not yet fixed): `BrandingController.php:71` defaults a missing `social_links` to PHP `[]`, which JSON-encodes as an **array**, while `storefront/src/lib/branding.ts:27`'s zod schema expects an **object** (`.nullable()`) — a one-line fix (`?? []` → `?: null`), not yet applied.

**Session summary, 2026-08-27 (new session) — Realtime broadcasting (Reverb + Echo) and queue-driver-to-Redis + Horizon + Pulse grilled and recorded as [ADR-047](./adr.md) and [ADR-048](./adr.md); nothing built yet.** Founder confirmed Laravel Reverb as non-negotiable going in; grilling scoped *how* (channel design for guest-checkout storefront vs. authenticated admin, conversion sequencing across the 5 real polling loops found live in the codebase, deployment shape) and surfaced a second, related decision (Horizon needs `QUEUE_CONNECTION=redis`, which fires a revisit trigger `ADR-020` decision #3 had already named in advance) plus a real money-adjacent risk caught before it shipped: the provisioned `redis` container runs with persistence fully disabled (`--save "" --appendonly no`, correct for cache-only use) — moving the queue onto it as-is would risk losing real unprocessed jobs (a paid order's `FulfillOrderJob`) on any Redis restart. ADR-048 decision 2 closes this (`--appendonly yes`) as part of the same change, not a follow-up. Full detail in both ADRs — not duplicated here. Pulse adopted alongside Horizon (same `/middleware` placement/admin-gate); Precognition and Pennant evaluated and **not** adopted (no confirmed present trigger for either) — see the pointer below.

**Realtime Broadcasting (ADR-047, items 1-3, 2026-08-27):** ✅ `laravel/reverb`/`laravel-echo`/`pusher-js` — `OrderStatusUpdated` (storefront, public `order.{order_number}` channel, `OrderObserver` seam), `PriceSyncRunUpdated` (admin, private `price-sync-run.{id}`, `PriceSyncRunObserver`), `BackupRunUpdated` (admin, private `backups`, `BackupRunObserver`); `docker-compose.prod.yml` `reverb` service + nginx `/app/` proxy; `NEXT_PUBLIC_REVERB_*` build-arg wiring in both Next.js Dockerfiles + CI's `build-and-push`. Not yet real-verified: actual `reverb:start` runtime and the nginx WebSocket handshake — no `.env` access in the build session, build-only (`docker compose build`) verification. **Founder must confirm live before/at first real deploy**, not assumed working from a clean build alone.

**Session summary, 2026-08-27 (new session) — ADR-048 (Queue → Redis, Horizon, Pulse) built end-to-end.** `QUEUE_CONNECTION=redis`, `config/horizon.php`'s three per-queue supervisors (replacing the three `queue-worker-*` containers with one `horizon` service), Redis AOF persistence (`docker-compose.prod.yml`), Pulse adopted alongside. **Grilled and closed live, mid-build, a real gap the ADR's own decision 3 undersold:** this backend has no session-based login anywhere (Sanctum bearer-token-only), so Horizon/Pulse's own `web`-guard dashboards had nothing to authenticate against — a deliberate, scoped exception to the very precedent `ADR-047` decision 3 had just set the same day (avoiding the `web` guard entirely for broadcasting auth). Closed via a signed-URL session bootstrap (`OpsAccessController` — mint a 5-minute signed link from inside the authenticated SPA, visiting it is the *only* place this backend ever calls `Auth::guard('web')->login()`), not a standing login form. Full detail in [ADR-048's build addendum](./adr.md) — not duplicated here. Local dev gained a real new prerequisite (`backend/docker-compose.yml`'s new `redis` service, `docker compose up -d redis` before `composer run dev`) since local dev never previously needed Docker running at all; `AGENTS.md` updated. 901/901 backend suite green (9 new tests, `OpsAccessControllerTest`). `admin/` `tsc --noEmit`/lint clean. **Not done — founder's own next step:** `QUEUE_CONNECTION=redis` still needs setting in the real `backend/.env` (local) and production/staging `.env` files; this assistant's `.env`/`.env.example` file access is permission-blocked. Decision 2's `kill -9`-restart-survival smoke test also still needs running against a real deploy, per the ADR's own consequence-to-track.

**Founder live-testing, same day — one real bug found: `/pulse` 500'd on first real load, first diagnosis wrong, corrected same session.** `Illuminate\Support\Collection` unserialize error. First fix attempt (pointing Pulse's cache driver at `redis`) didn't actually fix it — real cause was `config/cache.php`'s `serializable_classes => false` (a Laravel security default against gadget-chain attacks, a single global setting regardless of store) silently downgrading every cached object to `__PHP_Incomplete_Class`; `laravel/pulse` doesn't account for this newer framework default, a real open upstream gap found via `WebSearch`. Fixed with a narrow allowlist (4 exact types Pulse's source actually caches), not a blanket `true`. Horizon itself tested live and worked correctly first try — real queue job dispatched and observed completing in the dashboard. Full corrected detail in [ADR-048's build addendum](./adr.md).

Investigation before grilling found all three already overlapped shipped screens (Admin Dashboard's System Health, `/middleware/request-logs`, `/middleware/suppliers`, `/admin/games`, the storefront/resend flow's existing validate-player call) — none needed the full original scope. Live click-through verification via Chrome automation could not complete this session (mouse clicks stopped registering on the automated tab entirely, an environment/extension issue unrelated to this change; ref-click, coordinate-click, keyboard Tab+Space/Enter, and a full reload were all tried) — **founder manually verified `/middleware` and the new Validators panel live in the browser instead, confirmed working.** PR #16 opened against `staging`.

**Session summary, 2026-08-28 (new session) — Reviews (REV-1..5) grilled and built end-to-end as [ADR-053](./adr.md), on `feature/reviews-management`.** Founder picked this as the next backlog item; grilled from scratch since the PRD's own REV-1..5 wording covers only admin moderation, nothing about how a review actually gets created (guest checkout, ADR-011, no accounts). Real design work: submission gated on `delivery_status=Delivered`, `order_number` as the sole proof of ownership (same trust model as `TrackOrderController`), one review per order (`reviews.order_id` unique index). The load-bearing find: `storefront/src/components/order/OrderStatusTracker.tsx` (ADR-047) already unifies a Reverb push and a poll fallback into one `order` state object — the new review popup (`RateOrderModal.tsx`, deliberately named apart from the pre-existing, unrelated checkout `ReviewModal.tsx`) hooks directly into that existing state via a derived `delivered && !has_review` condition, no new real-time plumbing. `has_review` (new field on `TrackOrderController`'s response and `OrderStatusUpdated`'s broadcast payload) makes "don't show the popup again" a server-truth check, not client-side dismiss-forever state — a customer who closes the popup gets asked again on any later visit, from any device, until they actually submit. Admin side: `/admin/reviews` (stat cards, filterable table, approve/reject, bulk-approve), `average_rating` scoped to approved-only reviews (decision 7) so a rejected review never skews it. No public display anywhere (decision 6) — `TestimonialsSection.tsx`'s static content stays untouched, deliberately deferred to a future ADR if the founder ever wants it. 24 new backend tests (966/966 backend suite green), `admin/`+`storefront/` `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint`/`next build` all clean. **Live browser verification, same day (dev servers already running) — full flow confirmed end-to-end, one real bug found and fixed.** Created a throwaway `is_test=true` order (`KRS-QAREVIEW1`, `delivery_status=delivered`) and a temp QA admin (both deleted after). Confirmed: the `RateOrderModal` popup auto-opens on `/order/status/[orderNumber]` load for a delivered order with no review; star selection + comment + submit works; the popup auto-closes and **does not reappear on reload** (`has_review` server-truth confirmed); the submitted review lands in `/admin/reviews` as `pending` with correct game/package/customer/comment; Approve/Reject/status filter all work live, and `average_rating` correctly recomputes approved-only (confirmed excluding a rejected 1-star from the average) — decision 7 verified against real data, not just the test suite.

**Real bug found: `Admin\ReviewController`'s "Approve All Pending" button used a native `window.confirm()`** — the only screen in this codebase to do so (every other confirm-style action here uses an in-app `Modal`, e.g. `CreateBlacklistEntryModal`'s pattern). Beyond being inconsistent with this codebase's own convention, a native dialog blocks all further browser events, including the automated verification session's own click dispatch (matches this tool's own documented "if you accidentally trigger a dialog and lose responsiveness" warning). Fixed same session: replaced with a proper in-app confirm `Modal` (`confirmingBulkApprove` state, Cancel/Approve All buttons), re-verified live — works correctly, no freeze, same stats-recalculation behavior confirmed a second time (`Pending 0 / Approved 3 / Rejected 1 / Total 4 / Average 4`).
